# 2026 Changelog

### ClickHouse release v26.11.1.2942-stable (5a7822635c6) FIXME as compared to v26.11.1.1-new (3e724bd16d4)

#### Backward Incompatible Change
* Removed the per-query filesystem cache write limit, which capped how much a single query could write into one filesystem cache. Its accounting never reflected what a query had actually written, and it cost a second priority queue per query on the cache reservation hot path. The settings `filesystem_cache_max_download_size` and `filesystem_cache_skip_download_if_exceeds_per_query_cache_write_limit` (with its alias `skip_download_if_exceeds_query_cache`), and the cache configuration setting `enable_filesystem_query_cache_limit`, are now obsolete and ignored, so queries and disk definitions that set them keep working. It is recommended to just use SLRU cache policy instead. [#114165](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114165) ([Kseniia Sumarokova](https://github.com/kssenii)).
* `hash` and `parallel_hash` are now one join implementation. `parallel_hash` is an obsolete alias of `hash`: it is removed from the default `join_algorithm`, still accepted, and reads back as `hash`. A hash join now picks its layout from `parallel_hash_join_threshold` and the estimated size of the right table, not from the algorithm name. Below the threshold, one thread builds the hash table as one part. At or above it, and without an estimate, the join splits the hash table into parts that up to `max_threads` threads fill. The largest value always builds one part. The new setting `hash_join_layout` (`auto` by default, `serial` or `parallel`) forces the layout, for tests and debugging. Joins with `OR` in `ON` can build in parallel. A join built in parallel can return rows in a different order and pick a different row for `ANY`; use `ORDER BY` for a stable order. `EXPLAIN` shows `HashJoin` instead of `ConcurrentHashJoin`; the profile events `HashJoinBuiltWithParallelLayout` / `HashJoinBuiltWithSerialLayout` show which layout a hash table was built with. `used_join_algorithms` in `system.query_log` shows `HASH` for every hash join. The `ConcurrentHashJoinPoolThreads*` metrics are replaced by `HashJoinDestroyThreads*`. [#115605](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115605) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* By default (when `--path` is not specified), `clickhouse-local` now stores its data in a designated directory inside the home directory (`$XDG_DATA_HOME/clickhouse-local` or `~/.local/share/clickhouse-local` on Linux, `~/Library/Application Support/clickhouse-local` on macOS), so the data survives between runs. The previous behavior of using a unique temporary directory that is removed on exit is available with the new `--tmp` option. Only one instance can work with the same directory at a time: for concurrent instances, use `--tmp` or `--path`. [#116042](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116042) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reading the columns of `system.tables` that describe a table's schema requires the `SHOW COLUMNS` privilege on that table, the same privilege `DESCRIBE` and `SHOW CREATE TABLE` require: `create_table_query`, `engine_full`, `as_select`, `partition_key`, `sorting_key`, `primary_key`, `sampling_key`, `unique_key`, `skipping_indices_types` and `parameterized_view_parameters`. Previously any column-level grant, such as `GRANT SELECT(col) ON db.t`, sufficed to read the full DDL of `db.t`. For a table created by `CREATE DICTIONARY` the `CREATE` query follows `SHOW CREATE DICTIONARY` and stays readable with `SHOW DICTIONARIES`; the row itself is still listed only with `SHOW TABLES`. The columns naming other tables (`dependencies_*`, `loading_dependencies_*`, `loading_dependent_*`, `target_database`, `target_table`) now list only tables the user may see with `SHOW TABLES`. `INFORMATION_SCHEMA.VIEWS.view_definition` follows the same rule. To restore the previous behaviour, grant `SHOW COLUMNS ON db.t`, the same on an `Alias` target, and `SHOW TABLES` on the tables the dependency columns name. [#117448](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117448) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `extractGroups` now rejects a regular expression with more than 127 capturing groups, the same limit `extractAllGroupsHorizontal` and `extractAllGroupsVertical` have always enforced. A pattern with 128 or more capturing groups that previously returned results now throws `BAD_ARGUMENTS`. In the Kusto dialect, `extract(regexp, 0, text)` adds one group internally, so its limit is 126. To keep such a pattern working, use non-capturing groups `(?:...)` for the groups whose values are not needed. [#117510](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117510) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `CREATE TABLE x AS src` inherits the credentials embedded in the engine of `src`, which are masked in `SHOW CREATE TABLE`. It now requires the `SELECT` privilege on `src` when the inherited definition can carry credentials, instead of only `SHOW COLUMNS`. [#118866](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118866) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* `LOCAL` between a table and a join, as in `SELECT * FROM a LOCAL JOIN b ON a.k = b.k`, is now parsed as the join locality qualifier, like `GLOBAL`, instead of being silently taken as an implicit alias of `a`. A query that relies on such an implicit `local` alias, for example `SELECT * FROM t local JOIN u ON local.id = u.id`, now fails; write `t AS local` instead. Stored view and table definitions are not affected, because they are saved with an explicit `AS`. [#120222](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120222) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Azure Blob Storage access from user SQL no longer uses the server's own workload or managed identity unless `allow_server_credentials_in_user_queries` (formerly `s3_allow_server_credentials_in_user_queries`) is enabled; provide explicit credentials instead: `account_name` and `account_key`, a connection string, or a SAS token. [#120343](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120343) ([Smita Kulkarni](https://github.com/SmitaRKulkarni)).
* The function `toStartOfInterval` (and its aliases `time_bucket` and `date_bin`) now returns a value of the same type as its argument (`Date`, `Date32`, `DateTime`, or `DateTime64` with the same scale) instead of a type determined by the interval unit, which wrapped values outside the range of `Date` and `DateTime`. For example, `toStartOfInterval(toDate32('1900-01-01'), INTERVAL 1 DAY)` returned `2036-02-07 06:28:16` and now returns `1900-01-01`. Set `to_start_of_interval_preserves_argument_type = 0` to restore the previous behavior, in particular for tables whose partition or sorting key uses `toStartOfInterval` with a unit that changes the result type. [#122129](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122129) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `CREATE TABLE` and `ALTER TABLE` now reject an alias in `PARTITION BY`, `PRIMARY KEY`, `ORDER BY`, `UNIQUE KEY`, `SAMPLE BY`, the table and column `TTL`, skip index (also via an `ALIAS` column it uses) and constraint expressions with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`; write the expression without the alias. Such an alias was accepted before but could break the table: `PRIMARY KEY (c0 AS a)` without `ORDER BY` stored a definition that could not be read back, so the server could not start after a restart; an alias in `PARTITION BY`, in a skip index or in a `TTL ... RECOMPRESS` expression made every `INSERT` fail; and a skip index alias naming another column gave wrong results. Tables that already store such an alias keep loading, except one stored as `ORDER BY (expr AS alias)`, which could never be read back. Closes [#103115](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/103115). [#123053](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123053) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A writable disk with `table_disk = true` now holds the data of only one table: creating or attaching another table on the same path of the same storage, or on a path inside or above it, throws `BAD_ARGUMENTS`; tables over a read-only disk on that path are still allowed. Previously such tables returned each other's rows, lost inserts and deleted each other's files. A table dropped or detached without `SYNC` keeps its disk until its instance is gone. An `ALTER` can no longer change the disk of such a table. If existing metadata has two such writers, the table loaded second fails to load: before upgrading, recreate all but one of them over a read-only disk. Closes [#98512](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/98512). [#124298](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124298) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A filesystem cache with the `SLRU` policy (the default) now requires `max_file_segment_size <= max_size * min(slru_size_ratio, 1 - slru_size_ratio)` (of each part with `use_split_cache`), so that a file segment fits whole into each SLRU queue. With the default settings, a cache smaller than 80 MiB must set a smaller `max_file_segment_size` (and `boundary_alignment`); otherwise creating the cache disk, server startup or config reload fails. This also applies when an existing table with such a cache disk is attached. [#125817](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125817) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).
* `kill_throw_if_noop` now defaults to `true`. A `compatibility` level below `26.11` keeps the previous default. [#125968](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125968) ([Ye Yuan](https://github.com/Kryst4lskyxx)).
* Iceberg `time` columns read from Parquet data files are now returned as microseconds since midnight, as documented, instead of whole seconds. The value now agrees with the manifest statistics and partition values, so `WHERE` on such a column no longer misses rows and the unit no longer depends on the partition spec. Closes [#118807](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/118807). Closes [#88063](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/88063). [#126079](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126079) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `enable_hard_links` setting of disks with the `plain_rewritable` metadata is enabled by default, so `ALTER` operations and mutations of `MergeTree` tables are supported on such disks. After the first hard link is created on a disk (by a mutation, an `ALTER`, a `FREEZE` or a backup), the disk is not readable by servers older than `26.10`. Set `enable_hard_links` to `0` in the disk configuration to keep the previous behavior. [#126226](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126226) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `icebergTruncate` now returns `UInt64` for a `UInt64` value; previously it returned `Int64`, so results of 2^63 and more came back negative, also as MergeTree partition IDs. It is also no longer treated as monotonic for `Int64`, whose lowest values wrap around, so filters on its argument no longer skip parts or Iceberg files holding them. After the upgrade, tables created by a previous version that use `icebergTruncate` of a `UInt64` column in a partition key, sorting key or skipping index can have parts detached as broken on start or return wrong results. Closes [#125948](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125948). [#126302](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126302) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `parseDateTime` now rejects ISO week 53 for ISO week-years that have only 52 weeks, instead of silently normalizing those dates into the following calendar year. [#126356](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126356) ([Henry Su](https://github.com/hsusul)).
* A `vector_similarity` index now rejects `hnsw_max_connections_per_layer` greater than `512` and `hnsw_candidate_list_size_for_construction` greater than `3200`, because building an index with a huge `hnsw_max_connections_per_layer` could get the server killed by the OOM killer. Existing tables with larger values no longer load after upgrading: drop such indexes and recreate them with supported values before upgrading. [#126482](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126482) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `INSERT ... FROM INFILE` in `clickhouse-client` and `clickhouse-local` inserts only the data from the file and no longer also inserts the data piped to stdin. This also fixes the query waiting for an open stdin pipe to close. [#126564](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126564) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* An `INSERT` into a non-empty file through the `File` table engine or the `file` table function now fails with `CANNOT_APPEND_TO_FILE` for `JSONCompact`, `JSONCompactStrings`, `ProtobufList`, `ProtobufSingle`, for `JSONEachRow` and its aliases with `output_format_json_array_of_rows = 1`, for `Template` with a non-empty `format_template_rows_between_delimiter` (the default is `\n`) or a result-set format with anything after `${data}`, and for `CustomSeparated` with a non-empty `format_custom_row_between_delimiter`. Previously the `INSERT` succeeded, and reads in that format returned only the first `INSERT`'s rows or failed. To keep writing to such files, use `engine_file_truncate_on_insert = 1` or `engine_file_allow_create_multiple_files = 1`; for `Template`, set `format_template_rows_between_delimiter = ''` and end the row format with `\n`. Closes [#126614](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/126614). [#126699](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126699) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Tables with the `S3`, `AzureBlobStorage`, `HDFS` and data lake engines take format settings (e.g. `format_csv_delimiter`) from the server's settings and the table's `SETTINGS` clause, as `File` and `URL` do, and no longer from the session that creates them. Before, the session's values applied only until the table was loaded again, e.g. on server restart. To keep a format setting, state it in the table's `SETTINGS` clause. [#126987](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126987) ([Vadim Lopatin](https://github.com/buggins)).
* Added the `AI FUNCTIONS` privilege group and per-function privileges (`aiGenerate`, `aiEmbed`, etc.) that control who can call AI functions, e.g. `GRANT AI FUNCTIONS ON *.* TO role` or `GRANT aiGenerate, aiClassify ON *.* TO role`. Calling an AI function now requires its privilege in addition to `NAMED COLLECTION` access to the selected credentials. To keep existing users working, run `GRANT AI FUNCTIONS ON *.* TO <user_or_role>`. Functions used in the keys, TTL, skip indexes and projections of a new `MergeTree` table now require the privileges of the user creating the table. AI functions are rejected in newly defined `MergeTree` keys, TTL expressions, skip indexes and projections, in `DEFAULT` and `MATERIALIZED` expressions computed in the background, and in mutations, even when the user has the AI privilege; use a lightweight `UPDATE` or `INSERT ... SELECT` instead of `ALTER TABLE ... UPDATE` to fill a column with AI function results. [#127057](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127057) ([George Larionov](https://github.com/george-larionov)).
* Removed the experimental functions `detectCharset` and `detectLanguageUnknown`, because the data they relied on has an unknown license. Use `detectLanguage` for language detection. [#127086](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127086) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Setting `after_processing_move_uri` or `after_processing_move_connection_string` of an `S3Queue` or `AzureQueue` table now requires `WRITE ON S3` for the destination URL (or `WRITE ON AZURE`), and `ALTER TABLE ... MODIFY SETTING` also requires `SELECT` on the table. Before, the `ALTER SETTINGS` grant was enough. The destination must also be allowed by `remote_url_allow_hosts`. [#127180](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127180) ([Antonio Andelic](https://github.com/antonio2368)).
* `REVOKE <privilege> ON <source>('<regexp>')` now revokes only the grant with exactly this source filter. Previously, it revoked all grants of the privilege on the source, including other filters or the grant without a filter. To revoke everything on a source, use `REVOKE <privilege> ON <source>` without a filter. Revoking a filter that produces a partial revoke now throws an exception. [#127223](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127223) ([Pervakov Grigorii](https://github.com/GrigoryPervakov)).
* Tables with the `S3`, `AzureBlobStorage`, `HDFS` and data lake engines take format settings (e.g. `format_csv_delimiter`) from the server's settings and the table's `SETTINGS` clause, as `File` and `URL` do, and no longer from the session that creates them. Before, the session's values applied only until the table was loaded again, e.g. on server restart. To keep a format setting, state it in the table's `SETTINGS` clause. [#127491](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127491) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A storage policy can no longer contain two disks that store data in the same directory, such as several `cache` disks over one disk, a `cache` disk together with the disk it wraps, or an `encrypted` disk without its own `path` over one of them. Such a policy makes every storage policy lookup fail with `EXCESSIVE_ELEMENT_IN_CONFIG`, so a server with existing tables, system log tables included, does not start, whatever policies the tables use. Before, a `MergeTree` table on such a policy found each of its parts twice on restart and deleted them as duplicates, losing the data. Before upgrading, keep only one of these disks in each policy; the parts stay readable through it. Closes [#127791](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127791). [#127796](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127796) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The install script (`curl https://clickhouse.com/ | sh`) now downloads the newest release branch instead of `master`. To get the `master` build as before, run `curl https://clickhouse.com/ | CLICKHOUSE_BRANCH=master sh`. `CLICKHOUSE_BRANCH` also accepts a branch such as `26.9`. [#128272](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128272) ([Alexei Fedotov](https://github.com/leshikus)).
* The compression method of `file` (the explicit argument or the one detected from the file name) now applies to a file inside an archive, like for a plain file, e.g. `file('a.tar :: data.json.gz')` is decompressed. Previously the argument was ignored for archives, so `file('x.tar.gz :: a.csv', CSV, 'auto', 'gzip')` worked and now fails because `a.csv` is not gzip-compressed; omit the argument in that case. Also fixed format detection for a glob inside a tar archive. [#128438](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128438) ([Henry Su](https://github.com/hsusul)).
* The legacy Delta Lake reader was removed, and Delta Lake tables are always read with `delta-kernel-rs`. The setting `allow_delta_kernel_rs` (`allow_experimental_delta_kernel_rs`) is obsolete and has no effect. Delta Lake tables on GCS that rely on `http_client = 'gcp_oauth'` and Azure blobs with a leading `/` in their names are no longer readable. [#128445](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128445) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `CREATE HANDLER`, `ALTER HANDLER`, `DROP HANDLER` and `SHOW HANDLERS` now form the new `HANDLER ADMIN` privilege group and are no longer part of the `CREATE`, `ALTER` and `DROP` groups. Previously `GRANT CREATE ON *.*` or `GRANT ALTER ON *.*` was enough to define or rewrite an HTTP handler, whose query runs with the privileges of the user who calls it. Users who manage handlers must now be granted `HANDLER ADMIN` or the individual handler privileges, including users whose stored grants name only the old groups: for example, `GRANT CREATE, CREATE HANDLER` is stored as `GRANT CREATE`, and `GRANT ALL` followed by `REVOKE SYSTEM` is stored as a list of groups. Revoking `CREATE`, `ALTER` or `DROP` no longer revokes them, so a configured grant list that revokes one of these from `ALL` should also revoke `HANDLER ADMIN`. [#129033](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129033) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Servers older than 23.3 can no longer exchange distributed queries with newer servers over a cluster with a `<secret>`: the legacy interserver mode without a nonce has been removed. [#129053](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129053) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* The `max_distributed_connections` setting no longer accepts 0. Setting it to 0 in a query, a session or a settings profile now fails with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`, and a server whose users configuration sets it to 0 in a profile does not start (`CANNOT_LOAD_CONFIG`); use 1 instead. Background inserts into a `Distributed` table that an older version queued with 0 are not sent after the upgrade and block the files queued after them, so run `SYSTEM FLUSH DISTRIBUTED` before upgrading. Before, 0 made a synchronous `INSERT` into a `Distributed` table or into the `remote` table function hang forever, which also blocked server shutdown when the insert came from an asynchronous insert flush. [#129262](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129262) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Floating-point values with magnitude 2^63 or more are now converted exactly to `Int128`, `UInt128`, `Int256`, `UInt256`, `Decimal128` and `Decimal256`, and the result is the same on every platform. Previously, for example, `toInt128(1e20)` returned `100000000000000000003`, `accurateCast(1e20, 'Int128')` threw an exception and `toInt128(-1.7014118346046923e38)` returned a positive number. The low digits of these conversions change, including `Decimal128`/`Decimal256` with a large scale (for example `toDecimal128(14.0, 20)`), where the value multiplied by 10^scale exceeds 2^63. If a partition key, sorting key, primary key or skip index of a table converts a float this way, parts written by older versions keep the old key values: re-insert the rows of affected partitions and of parts whose sorting key converts out-of-range values or the `Int128` minimum (their rows are no longer in key order), and rewrite parts with other affected sorting keys or skip indexes using `ALTER TABLE ... REWRITE PARTS`. [#129277](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129277) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Setting `count_distinct_optimization` is obsolete and has no effect. It rewrote `count(DISTINCT x)` into `count()` over a `GROUP BY x` subquery, which is no longer faster than the radix partitioned `uniqExact` used for `count(DISTINCT x)` without `GROUP BY` (`optimize_uniq_exact_radix_partitioning`). [#129307](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129307) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* ClickHouse refuses to start if shared objects are injected into its process with `/etc/ld.so.preload`, `ld.so --preload`, or auditing libraries. Previously only the `LD_PRELOAD` and similar environment variables were handled, by removing them and re-executing. [#129501](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129501) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `CREATE WORKLOAD`, `CREATE RESOURCE`, `DROP WORKLOAD` and `DROP RESOURCE` are no longer part of the `CREATE` and `DROP` privilege groups and have to be granted explicitly (`GRANT ALL` still includes them). Previously `GRANT CREATE ON *.*` was enough to create a `default` workload that throttles the queries of every user. Users who manage workloads and resources through `GRANT CREATE` or `GRANT DROP` need `GRANT CREATE WORKLOAD, CREATE RESOURCE, DROP WORKLOAD, DROP RESOURCE ON *.*`. This includes grants saved before the upgrade: `GRANT CREATE, CREATE WORKLOAD` was saved as `GRANT CREATE`, and `GRANT ALL ON *.*` followed by a revoke on `*.*` as a list of groups. A `users.xml` grant list that revokes `CREATE` or `DROP` from `ALL` should revoke these privileges as well. [#129772](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129772) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Removed support for files in the async insert queue of `Distributed` tables written by ClickHouse versions older than 19.18. Such files are moved to the `broken` directory instead of being sent. [#129950](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129950) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* MongoDB connection strings can no longer make the server authenticate with its own credentials: the `MONGODB-OIDC`, `MONGODB-AWS`, `GSSAPI` and `MONGODB-X509` authentication mechanisms and the `tlsCertificateKeyFile` option. In the `mongodb` table function and `MongoDB` tables they are refused unless `s3_allow_server_credentials_in_user_queries` is enabled for the session that reads. In MongoDB dictionaries created with a DDL query they are always refused; define such dictionaries in the server configuration files instead. Existing tables and dictionaries that use them stop working. [#129969](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129969) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* You can now drop a cached Vector Similarity Index for a specific table: `SYSTEM DROP VECTOR SIMILARITY INDEX CACHE [ON CLUSTER c] [FOR TABLE [db.]table]` , while the indexes of other tables stay cached. [#130325](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130325) ([Yan Alexandre](https://github.com/yan-alex)).
* Forbid complex engines (`EmbededRocksDB`, `Replicated*`, `KeeperMap`, ...) for `CREATE TEMPORARY TABLE`. [#130554](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130554) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* A `vector_similarity` index can no longer be created (`CREATE TABLE`, `ALTER TABLE ... ADD INDEX`, `CREATE INDEX`) on a `MergeTree`-family engine other than `MergeTree` and `ReplicatedMergeTree`, e.g. `ReplacingMergeTree` or `CollapsingMergeTree`, because `SELECT ... FINAL` that uses such an index may return row versions that were already replaced or deleted. Existing tables keep working. To create such an index anyway, set the MergeTree setting `allow_vector_similarity_index_in_specialized_merge_tree = 1` (or `compatibility` to `26.10` or lower in the default profile). [#130572](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130572) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* What a query runs under the identity of a `SQL SECURITY DEFINER` or `NONE` view or materialized view is now logged in `system.query_log` as a child row (`is_initial_query = 0`, the query's `initial_query_id`, the definer as `user`) with the tables, columns, partitions and projections it accessed; the caller's row no longer lists the tables such a materialized view writes. Reads of a serialized query plan record the tables they read. `system.user_query_log` returns only top-level (`is_initial_query = 1`) rows of the authenticated matching user. A row policy that exposes `system.query_log` rows by user (`user`, `initial_user` or `authenticated_user`) should add `is_initial_query = 1` to keep the child rows hidden. [#130654](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130654) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Update the experimental `ZXC` codec to `zxc` 1.0.0 (format v9). Existing format-v8 `ZXC` data must be rewritten with another codec (or exported using the previous ClickHouse version) before upgrading. Older servers cannot read newly written format-v9 data; avoid mixed-version use of `ZXC` and rewrite new data before downgrading. [#131071](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131071) ([Actuele AI](https://github.com/actueleai)).
* Clients and servers with native protocol revision older than 54429 (ClickHouse releases from 2019 and older, or third-party drivers that announce such a revision) can no longer pass query settings in the old binary format. Queries without settings still work. [#131099](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131099) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Previously, each session accessing a persistent `S3`, `S3Queue`, `AzureBlobStorage`, or `AzureQueue` table could rebuild its shared client using that session's `s3_allow_server_credentials_in_user_queries` value. The table now keeps the credential-restriction mode used to build its client. A session where the setting is disabled cannot use an `S3` or `S3Queue` table created with it enabled, even with explicit credentials or `NOSIGN`; an Azure table is rejected only when its cached client uses the server's identity. Enabling the setting no longer restores server credentials to a table loaded anonymously after restart, and an `S3` table created with the setting disabled no longer picks up authentication changes from `<s3>` or endpoint configuration on each query. `DETACH` and `ATTACH` the table in a session with the intended setting after restart or credential rotation to rebuild its client. [#131104](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131104) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* A table of the `MergeTree` family can no longer get a column with the name of one of its virtual columns (for example `_part`, `_part_offset`, `_part_index` or `_table`) in `CREATE TABLE`, `ATTACH TABLE` with a full definition, or `ALTER TABLE ... ADD COLUMN` and `RENAME COLUMN`. Such a column hid the virtual column, which caused exceptions, crashes and wrong results. Existing tables are still loaded. The old behavior is available with the `allow_reserved_column_names` setting. [#131176](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131176) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Settings of type `Seconds` (for example `max_execution_time` or `lock_acquire_timeout`) now reject a negative value with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`. A negative `max_execution_time` meant no limit and passed a `MAX` constraint; a value whose microseconds round to `2^63` (for example `9223372036854.775`) was stored as a large negative number; it is rejected too. Before upgrading, remove negative values of these settings from configuration files, settings profiles, users and table definitions, and let `Distributed` async insert queues drain; otherwise the server can fail to start, a user or table fails to load, or a queued insert is never sent. [#131269](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131269) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The setting `distributed_ddl_entry_format_version` no longer accepts the values 1 and 2, and servers no longer execute distributed DDL entries written with those versions. Previously these versions sent `CREATE ... ON CLUSTER` to the hosts without normalizing it on the initiator, so each host resolved it with its own access. `CREATE TABLE ... ON CLUSTER` (and of views and dictionaries) on a cross-replication cluster, where replicas use different `default_database`, which only worked with these versions, is no longer supported; run the `CREATE` on each host instead. `CREATE TABLE ... ON CLUSTER ... CLONE AS` is now rejected, because only these versions copied the data; with the other versions it created an empty table. Older servers write such entries for the setting values 1, 2 and 3, so before upgrading set `distributed_ddl_entry_format_version` to at least 4 on all servers and wait until the distributed DDL queue is processed. The default value 5 is not affected. [#131402](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131402) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* The `SHOW FILESYSTEM CACHES` privilege is now checked: `SHOW FILESYSTEM CACHES`, `DESCRIBE FILESYSTEM CACHE` and `system.filesystem_cache_settings` require it. Before, it could be granted but was never checked, so any user could see the paths and settings of the filesystem caches. Users that need these statements should be granted it with `GRANT SHOW FILESYSTEM CACHES ON *.* TO <user>`; it is a global privilege now, so granting it on a database or a table is an error. [#131711](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131711) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).

#### New Feature
* Added the `CGroupMemoryInactiveFile` asynchronous metric, exposing inactive file-backed cgroup page cache for correlation with Kubernetes working-set reporting. [#98992](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/98992) ([Yash ](https://github.com/Onyx2406)).
* New `system.connections` to have visibility on active/idle clients connections to ClickHouse server. [#99702](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/99702) ([Kaviraj Kanagaraj](https://github.com/kavirajk)).
* Added new array combinatorics functions: `arrayPermutations`, `arrayPartialPermutations`, and `arrayCombinations`. These functions generate permutations and combinations by element indexes (so equal values may produce duplicate rows), validate argument `k`, and enforce result-size limits via `function_range_max_elements_in_block`. [#101135](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/101135) ([ArtemovMichael](https://github.com/ArtemovMichael)).
* Added the client flag --vim which emulates vim editing mode for the client. [#111218](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/111218) ([Isak Ellmer](https://github.com/spinojara)).
* Added support for reading and writing the [Vortex](https://github.com/vortex-data/vortex) columnar file format (the `Vortex` input and output format). This closes [#87327](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/87327). [#112950](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/112950) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Quotas can now be defined over any profile event by its name, e.g. `CREATE QUOTA network_egress FOR INTERVAL 1 day MAX S3GetObject = 100000, NetworkSendBytes = '100G' TO user1`, in both the SQL syntax and the `users.xml` configuration. The current consumption is reported in the new `profile_events` / `max_profile_events` Map columns of the `system.quota_usage`, `system.quotas_usage` and `system.quota_limits` tables. [#117004](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117004) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Exclude secrets (e.g. credentials, passwords, keys, tokens) from core dumps by allocating their memory with `MADV_DONTDUMP`. [#119331](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119331) ([Miсhael Stetsyuk](https://github.com/mstetsyuk)).
* Added `USER_PRIVILEGES`, `SCHEMA_PRIVILEGES`, `TABLE_PRIVILEGES` and `COLUMN_PRIVILEGES` views to `INFORMATION_SCHEMA`, so MySQL-compatible tools can introspect privileges. The views are built over `system.grants` and show positive, non-wildcard grants; grants on specific named objects of parameterized privilege types are omitted, while their `ON *` form is shown as a plain global privilege. [#121801](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121801) ([Nikolai Ovchinnikov](https://github.com/kolsha)).
* Puffin output format (iceberg v3 related feature). [#121982](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121982) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Added a `query_plan` column to `system.query_log`, containing the query plan that was executed with its per-step runtime statistics, serialized as `JSON`. Enabled with the `log_query_plans` setting. [#123059](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123059) ([Matheus Nerone](https://github.com/Manerone)).
* Support the Parquet `GEOMETRY` and `GEOGRAPHY` logical types: such columns are read as ClickHouse geo types, and `GeospatialStatistics` are used for spatial pruning. Geo columns can be written with the `GEOMETRY` logical type and `GeospatialStatistics` with the new setting `output_format_parquet_geometry_logical_type`. [#125131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125131) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Support the `ALP` encoding of floating-point columns in Parquet: such files can be read, and can be written with the new setting `output_format_parquet_use_alp_encoding`. [#125135](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125135) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The Prometheus HTTP API now supports the `/federate` endpoint, so a Prometheus server can scrape the latest samples of selected series from a `TimeSeries` table. [#125250](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125250) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Added the system table `system.iceberg_metadata_log_entries`, which lists the metadata files of Iceberg tables together with the latest snapshot id, schema id and sequence number at the time of each file. [#125639](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125639) ([Irfan Bunjaku](https://github.com/bunjj)).
* Include named collections in backups/restores. [#126028](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126028) ([Elian Gidoni](https://github.com/eliangidoni)).
* Add `INSERT INTO t ... RETURNING (SELECT ...)`, which runs a `SELECT` on top of the inserted data and streams its result while the data is being inserted, e.g. to get the values of columns generated on insertion. Inside the `SELECT`, the destination table (or the absence of `FROM`) refers to the inserted data. [#126476](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126476) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added functions `editDistanceRatio` (alias `levenshteinRatio`) and `stringSimilarityRatio` with UTF-8 variants, which return a normalized string similarity between 0 and 1. `stringSimilarityRatio` gives the same values as Python's `Levenshtein.ratio`. [#126499](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126499) ([sz-kristof](https://github.com/sz-kristof)).
* Added the `SHARED` modifier for typed paths of the `JSON` type, for example `JSON(SHARED a UInt32, SHARED b String)`. In `MergeTree` parts such paths are stored together in a constant set of files instead of a set of files per path, which keeps the number of files and object storage requests per part small for columns with many typed paths, while a read of a path still decodes only that path. The on-disk format is controlled by the new `MergeTree` setting `object_typed_shared_data_serialization_version`. Sub-column resolution of `JSON` now prefers a typed path over a same-named sub-column of another typed path, for example `json.a.b` of `JSON(a Tuple(b String), a.b Int64)` is now the typed path `a.b`; `MergeTree` rejects such types, so only other engines and `CAST` see the change. [#127000](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127000) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* `GDeflate` compression codec on the CPU. ~SOTA codec for GPU decompression - highly important for our future pipeline https://docs.nvidia.com/cuda/nvcomp/gdeflate.html. [#127422](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127422) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Added the `ssl_key_passphrase` argument so `ENGINE = MySQL`, the `mysql` table function, the MySQL database engine, and the MySQL dictionary source can use an encrypted client key (`ssl_key` / `ssl_key_pem`). [#127740](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127740) ([Young](https://github.com/ylw510)).
* Added functions `lengthCartesian`, `lengthSpherical` and `lengthSpheroid`, which return the length of `LineString`, `MultiLineString`, `Ring` and `Geometry` values on a plane, in radians on a sphere, and in meters on the WGS84 spheroid. `ST_Length` is an alias of `lengthCartesian`. Closes [#126453](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/126453). [#127764](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127764) ([Pulkit Chauhan](https://github.com/pulk17)).
* Added function `polygonMinimumRotatedRectangleCartesian`, which returns the rotated rectangle of the smallest area that contains a geometry, and functions `exteriorRing`, `interiorRingN` and `pointN` (with aliases `ST_ExteriorRing`, `ST_InteriorRingN` and `ST_PointN`), which take a ring of a polygon or a point of a line, including for the `Geometry` type. [#127944](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127944) ([Pulkit Chauhan](https://github.com/pulk17)).
* Add the `arrayTreePreorder(ids, parent_ids)` function. It lays out a tree stored as arrays of node ids and parent ids in depth-first preorder, with the subtree size, parent position and depth of each node, in linear time. This makes it possible to aggregate over subtrees (for example, the spans of a trace) with `arrayCumSum` or `arraySlice`. Cycles, orphans and duplicate ids do not cause an error. [#127980](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127980) ([Nikolai Kochetov](https://github.com/KochetovNicolai)).
* New server setting `filesystem_isolation`: at startup, the server moves into a new mount namespace where only the paths it needs according to its configuration are visible - in particular, no shell - which makes exploitation of a remote code execution harder. It is enabled as `best_effort` in the default `config.xml`; extra paths, such as interpreters for executable user defined functions, can be listed in `filesystem_isolation_paths`. [#128412](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128412) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the codec combinator `FirstApplicable`, which uses the first of the listed codecs that is applicable to the data type, e.g. `CODEC(FirstApplicable(PCO, ZSTD(3)))` compresses numbers with `PCO` and other data with `ZSTD(3)`. It can also be used where the data type is not known, e.g. `default_compression_codec = 'FirstApplicable(PCO, ZSTD(3))'`; the codec is then selected for the type of every column. An alternative can be a pipeline of codecs, written as `Multiple(T64, ZSTD(3))`. [#128473](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128473) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added the `JSONExtractLeafValues` and `JSONExtractLeafPathsAndValues` functions. They return the leaf values (and paths) of a JSON `String`, with an optional regular expression to skip subtrees, so a text index can use a `String` column directly. [#128710](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128710) ([rohit](https://github.com/rohityadav1993)).
* Support parallel parsing of the `Avro` format (Object Container Files) by splitting the file on block (sync marker) boundaries across threads. [#129682](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129682) ([Shaohua Wang](https://github.com/tiandiwonder)).
* Support writing Iceberg `variant` columns. [#130482](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130482) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Added ProfileEvents to estimate the effect of PREWHERE: `GranulesReadByPrewhereReaders`, `GranulesSkippedByPrewhereReaders`, and `RowsSkippedByPrewhereReaders`. [#130645](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130645) ([olegkkruglov](https://github.com/olegkkruglov)).
* Add the table setting `iceberg_timezone_for_timestamptz` to choose the time zone used for Iceberg `timestamptz` and `timestamptz_ns` columns. [#130772](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130772) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Add setting `output_format_arrow_uuid_as_fixed_byte_array` to write `UUID` in the `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` formats as plain `FixedSizeBinary(16)`, without the `arrow.uuid` extension type. [#130963](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130963) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Add setting `output_format_arrow_variant_as_json_string` to write `Variant` in the `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` formats as a string with the JSON representation of each value in a `Utf8` column instead of a dense union, for readers that do not support unions. [#130964](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130964) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added function `getQueryProfileEvent` which returns the current value of a profile event (such as `SelectedRows` or `ReadCompressedBytes`) for the current query. It is evaluated once per block, so it can be used to annotate the output or the inserted data with the progress of the query. [#131822](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131822) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).

#### Experimental Feature
* Added the experimental `SHUFFLE` clause, used in place of `ORDER BY`: `SELECT ... SHUFFLE [LIMIT n]` returns the rows in a uniformly random order (or a uniformly random sample of `n` rows) in O(n) time, in parallel and in distributed queries. Queries with `ORDER BY rand()` are executed the same way, up to ten times faster. Enable the clause with `allow_experimental_shuffle_query`. [#100305](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/100305) ([kaguya sama](https://github.com/xuir268)).
* With `make_distributed_plan` and `query_plan_optimize_lazy_final` both enabled, a `FINAL` query on a `ReplacingMergeTree` with a `WHERE` or `PREWHERE` no longer returns each row once per reader task. [#112493](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/112493) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The experimental distributed Cascades optimizer (`enable_cascades_optimizer`) now executes window functions with `PARTITION BY` on all nodes of the cluster: the input is shuffled by the partition keys and each node sorts and computes its partitions independently. [#116108](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116108) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* Switch the samples inner table of the TimeSeries engine to a bucketed schema:. [#117344](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117344) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* DPsub is now the default join order algorithm (`query_plan_optimize_join_order_algorithm = 'dpsub,greedy'`), together with the CD-C conflict detector, which also lets semi and anti joins take part in reordering. Queries with more than 12 joined tables fall back to the greedy algorithm. [#120110](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120110) ([Fisnik Kastrati](https://github.com/fkastrati)).
* A distributed query plan (`make_distributed_plan`) aggregates partially before it shuffles a `GROUP BY` by its keys, and merges the partial states per bucket, instead of shuffling every input row. Controlled by the new setting `distributed_plan_partial_aggregation_before_shuffle`. [#123537](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123537) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* //github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/blob/master/docs/Support CODEC declarations on individual stored Tuple elements behind enable_tuple_element_codecs. [#123966](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123966) ([Ilya Golshtein](https://github.com/ilejn)).
* Added the experimental `Elasticsearch` table engine and `elasticsearch` table function for reading documents from an Elasticsearch index as a `JSON` column, with the document id and index name available as virtual columns. Enable them with the `enable_elasticsearch` setting. [#124283](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124283) ([Kirill Kopnev](https://github.com/Fgrtue)).
* The columns cache (`use_columns_cache`) serves and stores whole cached columns without copying them, and aligns read blocks with its entries: on ClickBench, a cached scan of `SearchPhrase` is 10x faster with 32 threads and the run that fills the cache 1.8x faster. The memory of the cache now belongs to the server: a query that fills the cache is no longer charged for it, so it no longer fails `max_memory_usage` with data it no longer holds. [#124449](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124449) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Support `DROP TABLE` in the `DataLakeCatalog` database engine with the Unity catalog v2. [#124481](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124481) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)).
* The columns cache (`use_columns_cache`) now also applies to `Parquet` files read from the local filesystem, from object storages such as S3 and Azure, and by data lake tables. Repeated reads of the same row groups skip reading and decoding the column chunks. [#124531](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124531) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added `EXPLAIN PLAN fragments = 1`, which prints a plan executed by stateless workers (`make_distributed_plan = 1`) as one plan per query fragment, with the exchanges between the fragments and their levels. On a query that is not distributed it prints the usual plan, followed by the fallback reason when the planner fell back to local execution. [#124893](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124893) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* Add the setting `read_from_filesystem_cache_if_exists_otherwise_cache_temporarily` and the MergeTree setting `cache_temporarily_for_merges`: the experimental `ReaderExecutor` uses the filesystem cache as a temporary buffer and drops the segments it filled once it passes them, unless another reader holds them. [#125658](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125658) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).
* The experimental Cascades optimizer can run `DISTINCT` on several nodes of a distributed query plan: the preliminary `DISTINCT` deduplicates on every node before rows are gathered, and the final one runs on every node when its input is already split by the `DISTINCT` columns. [#126353](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126353) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* A query with `distributed_plan_execute_locally` that fails while starting the local tasks of a stage, for example on `max_memory_usage`, now returns the error instead of aborting the server. [#126696](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126696) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Added `EXPLAIN PLAN tree = 1`, which draws a plan executed by stateless workers (`make_distributed_plan = 1`) as boxes, one per step, framed by query fragment and connected by the exchanges between the fragments, followed by the list of exchanges and the levels of the fragments. [#126706](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126706) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* TimeSeries: Drop support for external targets. Setting `id_type` and `id_generator` were designed to help handling external targets, so they shouldn't be used anymore. [#126742](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126742) ([Vitaly Baranov](https://github.com/vitlibar)).
* Experimental transactions: a mutation that runs while a part's transaction metadata is being updated no longer copies the part's `txn_version.txt.tmp`, so it neither fails with `FILE_DOESNT_EXIST` on object storage nor produces a part that is dropped as rolled back on the next table load. [#127074](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127074) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The columns cache (`use_columns_cache`) now applies to compact parts of `MergeTree` tables, not only to wide parts. [#127287](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127287) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The columns cache (`use_columns_cache`) now applies to the reads of projections. [#127292](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127292) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Support `LowCardinality` columns in the experimental `UNIQUE KEY` of `MergeTree` tables. [#128122](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128122) ([johnjing](https://github.com/JingYanchao)).
* Introduces jitter to the metric_family and tags cache expiry for the TimeSeries table engine. [#128256](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128256) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* Improve concurrent inserts of new or expired time series into TimeSeries tables by batching deduplication-cache updates under one lock. <!-- CI automatic block start :ci_links: -->. [#128404](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128404) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* `OPTIMIZE` of Iceberg tables (`allow_experimental_iceberg_compaction`) now writes the `total-records`, `total-data-files` and `total-files-size` of every rewritten snapshot from its live rewritten files, so a trivial `count()` after compaction matches a full scan, and reads each position delete file once per data file it applies to instead of once for every snapshot that lists the two files, which makes compaction of long histories much cheaper. Closes [#128265](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/128265). [#128759](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128759) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The boxes of EXPLAIN PLAN tree = 1 now show the details EXPLAIN prints under each step, such as the keys of an aggregation or the parts and granules a read selects, and Expression steps are no longer drawn. [#128919](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128919) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* Switch PromQL `in` subqueries to use indexHints when we yield a range of metric IDs. [#129064](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129064) ([James Cunningham](https://github.com/JTCunning)).
* The settings of the steps of a serialized query plan (`serialize_query_plan`, distributed plans for stateless workers) are now sent by name with string values instead of a binary encoding, so a server skips a plan setting it does not know instead of rejecting the plan. This is incompatible with the previous versions: a serialized query plan cannot be exchanged with a server of version 26.10 or older. [#129973](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129973) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fix incorrect random sampling with SHUFFLE LIMIT when querying SQLite, PostgreSQL, or MySQL tables with limit pushdown enabled. [#130128](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130128) ([koz9va](https://github.com/koz9va)).
* `EXPLAIN PLAN fragments = 1, json = 1` now prints a plan executed by stateless workers (`make_distributed_plan = 1`) as one JSON document with the plan of every query fragment, the exchanges between the fragments and their levels, and the exchange id on every send and receive step. On a query that is not distributed it prints the same document as `json = 1`, with the fallback reason when the planner fell back to local execution. [#130337](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130337) ([Diego Gomes Tomé](https://github.com/diegomestre2)).
* The experimental Cascades optimizer does not shuffle the input rows of a `GROUP BY` when it has no estimate of the number of groups. It aggregates on each node and shuffles the aggregation states, unless the input is already distributed by a key. [#131094](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131094) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).

#### Performance Improvement
* `optimize_inverse_dictionary_lookup` now also rewrites `dictGet(...) IN (...)` and `dictGet(...) NOT IN (...)` with constant lists into a lookup of the matching dictionary keys, e.g. `WHERE dictGetString('dict', 'status', id) IN ('active', 'pending')` into `WHERE id IN (SELECT id FROM dictionary('dict') WHERE status IN ('active', 'pending'))`. This lets the primary key be used for filtering. [#100001](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/100001) ([euvre](https://github.com/euvre)).
* Optimize filtering by replacing columns constrained to a single value by WHERE equality conditions with ColumnConst. [#105541](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/105541) ([Yue Ni](https://github.com/niyue)).
* CountMin column statistics are now serialized into the part file without double buffering. [#109231](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/109231) ([Christoph Viebig](https://github.com/cv4g)).
* Runtime filters are now built on the equality keys of `ASOF INNER JOIN`, reducing rows entering the hash join. [#113667](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/113667) ([Antonio Álvarez Caballero](https://github.com/29antonioac)).
* Evaluate the final `DISTINCT` on several threads by repartitioning its input by the hash of the `DISTINCT` columns, instead of merging everything into a single stream and deduplicating it on one thread. Up to 6x faster on high-cardinality `DISTINCT`. Controlled by the new setting `allow_parallel_distinct`, enabled by default. Note that this changes the order in which a `DISTINCT` without `ORDER BY` returns rows, which was never defined. [#114550](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114550) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Improved the performance of parsing JSON objects into a `JSON` column. The per-key duplicate-key bookkeeping now keeps its two type sets as bit masks in a single map entry instead of two maps of nested sets. The gain grows with the number of keys per object, and applies whether or not the object contains a repeated key. [#115888](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/115888) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Merge `GROUP BY keys ORDER BY <aggregate> LIMIT n` queries with a threshold algorithm (Fagin): the final merge of parallel aggregation walks the per-thread hash tables in the order of the partial values of the ordering aggregate (`count`, `sum` of unsigned integers, `uniqExact`, `min`, `max`) and merges and materializes only the groups that can rank among the top `n` of each two-level bucket, instead of every group. Exact; controlled by the existing `query_plan_aggregation_bucket_top_k` setting, which covers both threshold merging and conversion-stage selection. [#116101](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116101) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Queries with `ARRAY JOIN` followed by `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` can run faster when the sort keys do not depend on expanded columns. The query planner can move bounded sorting below the expansion so only the top-n input rows are expanded, while the outer `LIMIT` still truncates the expanded output. For an inner `ARRAY JOIN`, an emptiness guard is inserted so rows whose arrays are all empty are filtered before the moved sort. The rewrite is controlled by `query_plan_top_k_through_array_join` and is disabled by default. [#116550](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116550) ([Young](https://github.com/ylw510)).
* Fixed a performance regression of `dictGet` with direct dictionaries (and of `x IN (<many literals>)` in general): constant literals are converted to the column type directly instead of through a CAST function per value. Closes [#115803](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/115803). [#117018](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117018) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Merging of large `uniqExact` states in `GROUP BY` queries (e.g. `SELECT k, COUNT(DISTINCT x) ... GROUP BY k`) is now parallelized, the same way it has long been for the without-key case. ClickBench Q8/Q9 hot runs get ~3x faster on many-core machines. [#117153](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117153) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Keeper serializes requests into nuraft log entries differently, with batching and more information stored. This increases throughput by 40% on some write-heavy workloads. Use coordination setting `use_batched_log_entries` to enable; all servers must first be updated to a version that supports it setting. [#117436](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117436) ([Michael Kolupaev](https://github.com/al13n321)).
* Reading `Array` and `Map` columns from Parquet is faster. The repetition and definition level loops in the native Parquet reader no longer update their output containers once per value, and a column with a single array level now produces its offsets, null map and encoded value count in one pass over the levels instead of two. Measured on x86_64 at 1M rows: 14% less CPU on `Array(Tuple(...))` and 19% less on `Map(String, Tuple(...))`. [#117627](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117627) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Conditions with AI functions (`aiGenerate`, `aiClassify`, `aiExtract`, `aiTranslate`, `aiFilter`, `aiRedact`, `aiEmbed`, `aiSimilarity`) are moved to the end of PREWHERE, so the AI function is called only for rows that pass the cheaper conditions. [#117802](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117802) ([George Larionov](https://github.com/george-larionov)).
* Disks that keep their metadata in the object storage itself (`plain`, `plain_rewritable`) or serve it from a web server (`web`, `web_index`) no longer run the background blob cleanup and blob replication tasks, which had nothing to do there. Previously every such disk woke up a background task once a second, which was noticeable with many read-only `MergeTree` tables, each on its own `plain_rewritable` disk. [#118662](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118662) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Improve phrase search performance when one of the phrase's terms is rare, because it skips the parts of the frequent terms' posting lists that cannot match. [#118885](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118885) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Add vectorized `memchr` and `strlen` implementations for x86_64 with runtime AVX-512 dispatch. [#118911](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118911) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Use the ARM optimized-routines string functions (`memcpy`, `memmove`, `memcmp`, `memchr`, `strlen`, `strcmp`, `strchr`, `strnlen`) on aarch64. [#118916](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118916) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* `row_number` now streams rows for its default window frame instead of buffering the entire partition when it is the only function in the window. [#120264](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120264) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Column statistics of `MergeTree` data parts are cached per data part and column in a new statistics cache (server settings `statistics_cache_size`, `statistics_cache_policy`, `statistics_cache_size_ratio`; `SYSTEM DROP STATISTICS CACHE`), loaded on demand and merged on the fly for every query. Queries over a pruned set of parts no longer re-read statistics from disk on every execution, and there is no background task that loads the statistics of all columns of all parts. The settings `refresh_statistics_interval` and `use_statistics_cache` are obsolete. A new merge-tree setting `prewarm_statistics_cache` optionally prewarms the cache for new parts and on startup. [#120758](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120758) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `hasAll` and `hasAny` over an `Array(LowCardinality(T))` column no longer expand the dictionary into plain values once per block. With a constant needle array whose element type is the dictionary's value type, each needle is resolved to a dictionary index once per block and every row is decided from those indexes. Measured on 10,000,000 rows with 8 needles in one thread: `hasAll` 1526 to 142 ms and `hasAny` 364 to 143 ms. Closes: [#120822](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120822). [#120987](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120987) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The disk that writes to a `plain_rewritable` location now publishes a compact snapshot of its metadata in `__meta/snapshot.bin`, and read-only disks pointing to the same location load their state from it on startup and on every refresh instead of listing the object storage and reading every `prefix.path`, which took tens of thousands of requests. Controlled by the disk settings `metadata_snapshot_enabled` and `metadata_snapshot_write_delay_ms`. Closes [#121416](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/121416). [#121431](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121431) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Functions `coalesce` and `ifNull` now use short-circuit evaluation: an argument is evaluated only for the rows where all the preceding arguments are `NULL`. [#121457](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121457) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Use automaton-guided dictionary seeks to skip unrelated text-index blocks for anchored `LIKE` and `ILIKE` patterns containing internal wildcards. [#121650](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121650) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* The query condition cache now also works for Parquet files read through table functions such as `file`, `s3` or `icebergS3`, not only through tables. [#122127](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122127) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Reduce memory usage and analysis time of filtered queries over wide tables with `bloom_filter`, `ngrambf_v1`, `tokenbf_v1`, `sparse_grams` or `text` skip indexes: the index condition no longer copies the names of all columns shaped like Map key subcolumns. [#122144](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122144) ([ClickGap AI Bot](https://github.com/clickgapai)).
* Queries to `system.clusters` are now much faster on servers with many `Replicated` databases: replica state is requested from Keeper concurrently in a bounded window instead of one round trip per database, and a `WHERE cluster = ...` condition is applied before going to Keeper, so only the selected clusters are queried. [#122223](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122223) ([Nikolai Ovchinnikov](https://github.com/kolsha)).
* A `JOIN ON` condition that combines an equality with an `OR` without equalities, such as `ON a.id = b.id AND (b.x IS NULL OR b.x < a.y)`, now keeps the equality as the join key of an `ALL INNER` join (and of an outer `ALL` join when a branch of the `OR` only compares both tables), so join reordering can use it. Before, the `OR` was expanded over the rest of the condition and join reordering could pick a near cross product: the query from the issue took 18.6 s instead of 0.04 s. Closes [#122510](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122510). [#122526](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122526) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Inserts into tables with materialized views no longer take a snapshot of all active parts of the source table for every view and every inserted block. This removes CPU overhead proportional to the number of parts multiplied by the number of views, for both synchronous and asynchronous inserts. [#122603](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122603) ([Michael Kolupaev](https://github.com/al13n321)).
* Lazy materialization now works for `LIMIT N` queries over JOINs. Controlled by the new setting `query_plan_lazy_materialization_for_join`, enabled by default. <!-- CI automatic block start :ci_links: -->. [#122682](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122682) ([Nikolai Kochetov](https://github.com/KochetovNicolai)).
* Fixed a performance regression where CSV parsing with `input_format_csv_missing_nullable_as_empty_string = 1` scaled badly with the number of parsing threads on `Nullable` columns with empty fields, due to per-field shared_ptr refcount contention. The check is now precomputed once per column instead of once per field. [#122821](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122821) ([Aditya Kumar](https://github.com/adityaksolves)).
* Faster compression and decompression with the `ZSTD` codec, most noticeably for incompressible data. [#122854](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122854) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* Improve performance of min, max, argMin, argMax and their -If variants on AArch64 CPUs with SVE (e.g., AWS Graviton3/Graviton4) via runtime-dispatched SVE implementations. [#122860](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122860) ([Chiranmoy Bhattacharya](https://github.com/chiranmoyf)).
* `hasAll` and `hasAny` are up to 2x faster on AArch64 for long arrays of 32 and 64-bit integers. [#122871](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122871) ([Chiranmoy Bhattacharya](https://github.com/chiranmoyf)).
* Text index direct read is faster for token searches whose tokens each match at most `text_index_posting_list_block_size` rows in a part (with the defaults, every token search on parts of up to about one million rows), outside the `lazy` posting list apply mode: the result column is filled once for all rows of a read from the posting list built during the index analysis, instead of once per granule. [#122896](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122896) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Lazy `FINAL` (`query_plan_optimize_lazy_final`) is much faster with `optimize_aggregation_in_order`: its deduplicating aggregation now merges groups in blocks bounded by `aggregation_in_order_max_block_bytes`, like an ordinary in-order `GROUP BY`, instead of one group at a time (25000 merges down to 3 on the reproducer from [#114579](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/114579)). Closes [#114579](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/114579). [#123326](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123326) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY` queries with many distinct keys run faster with the adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default). Across 13 ClickBench aggregation queries, speedups reach 2.9x, with geometric mean speedups of 1.62x at 64 threads and 1.47x at 16 threads. The adaptive aggregator's profile events are reworked to match: `AdaptiveAggregationGiveUps`, `AdaptiveAggregationPressureDrainedRecords`, `AdaptiveAggregationPressureStandDowns`, `AdaptiveAggregationPressureSweeps`, `AdaptiveAggregationResidueReleases`, `AdaptiveAggregationSealNormalizations`, `AdaptiveAggregationSealedChunks`, `AdaptiveAggregationSharedTableSpills`, `AdaptiveAggregationSpillBacklogSheds`, `AdaptiveAggregationStagedChunkPiecesOverBound`, `AdaptiveAggregationStagedChunkSplits`, `AdaptiveAggregationStagedClaimsClosedAtBound` and `AdaptiveAggregationStagedRecordsMerged` are removed, and `AdaptiveAggregationSpills`, `AdaptiveAggregationSpilledRecords`, `AdaptiveAggregationSpilledBytes`, `AdaptiveAggregationFrozenTableSpills`, `AdaptiveAggregationMergeUnits`, `AdaptiveAggregationPrunedUnits`, `AdaptiveAggregationPrunedRecords` and `AdaptiveAggregationCountFirstUnits` are added. [#123531](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123531) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* `count(DISTINCT x)` and `uniqExact(x)` without `GROUP BY` are faster and use less memory for high-cardinality columns: the values are partitioned by hash and each partition is deduplicated in parallel in a cache-resident hash set, instead of merging per-thread hash sets, and repeated runs of a query reuse the statistics of the first one. 2.8x faster on ClickBench Q4 and 1.7x on Q5 with 16 threads, up to 6x on high-cardinality columns, with 2x to 4x less memory. Controlled by the new setting `optimize_uniq_exact_radix_partitioning`. [#123552](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123552) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Lazy materialization is supported for `Memory` tables: for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` queries, the columns that are not needed for filtering and sorting are read only for the rows in the result. [#123788](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123788) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The query condition cache works for `Memory` tables: the granules of 8192 rows where no row satisfies the condition of a query are recorded, and later queries with the same condition skip them. [#123798](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123798) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `sum`, `avg` and `sumCount` of integer and `Decimal` columns without `GROUP BY` and filters are now answered from `basic` column statistics without reading the data, and so is `count(column)` in such queries. Controlled by the new setting `use_statistics_for_sum_avg_aggregation` (enabled by default). Parts written by older versions get the sum on their next merge. [#123950](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123950) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* In a PREWHERE condition on a single dictionary-encoded column of a Parquet file (strings and other values wider than 8 bytes), the functions applied to that column are now evaluated once per dictionary value instead of once per row, and only the rows that pass are materialized. ClickBench Q11 (`WHERE MobilePhoneModel <> ''`) over `hits.parquet` is about 22% faster. [#124131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124131) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Case-sensitive substring search (`LIKE '%...%'`, `position`, `countSubstrings` and similar) is faster on Arm CPUs with SVE, such as AWS Graviton 3, 4 and 5, thanks to a faster StringZilla SVE kernel that is now used at every SVE vector length. [#124136](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124136) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading Parquet columns after a `PREWHERE` step is faster, about 1.4x on ClickBench data when most rows pass: values of PLAIN-encoded fixed-size columns are copied in bulk instead of one virtual call per value, and a step that every row passes no longer filters the columns read so far. Closes [#124145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124145). [#124186](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124186) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading a Parquet file parses the metadata of only the columns that a query reads, and `count()` and schema inference parse none. The Parquet metadata cache shares the parsed column chunk metadata between queries instead of copying the whole file metadata for every query. On ClickBench's `hits.parquet`, `SELECT count()` in `clickhouse-local` is about 2.4 times faster. [#124364](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124364) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `bloom_filter` index is now used for LEFT ARRAY JOIN. [#124426](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124426) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Faster output formatting: writes of delimiters, short strings and numbers into output buffers no longer make a function call. Row-based text formats are 5-40% faster. [#124508](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124508) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* A `PREWHERE` condition on a subcolumn (for example `j.kind = 'b'` on a `JSON` column) is again evaluated in its own read step, so the rest of the column is read only for the rows that pass it. `m['key']` is now rewritten to the `Map` key subcolumn only for tables with `with_buckets` `Map` serialization, where it reads a single bucket; with `basic` serialization the whole `Map` is read once, as before 26.3. Closes [#124535](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124535). [#124553](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124553) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster `ORDER BY` over the result of a `JOIN` whose columns are replicated lazily (`enable_lazy_columns_replication`): merging these columns no longer pays a hash table lookup and a memory allocation for every row, and merged blocks without shared values are passed on as plain columns. The query from [#122436](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122436) became about 9 times faster, and sorting wide replicated values keeps the memory savings of lazy replication. [#124702](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124702) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* A single-element `IN` on a sorting key column, like `WHERE project IN ('p') ORDER BY ts LIMIT 10`, now lets the query read in order the same way `project = 'p'` does, instead of reading and sorting the whole range. [#124718](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124718) ([Luc Leray](https://github.com/lucleray)).
* Reading a text input that fits into one parallel parsing segment (for example a CSV, TSV or JSONEachRow file smaller than `min_chunk_bytes_for_parallel_parsing`) no longer starts a segmentation thread and a parsing task for it. Queries over many small files that use parallel parsing are about 30% faster. [#124806](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124806) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The query condition cache now also works for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` queries over data lake tables (`Iceberg`, `DeltaLake`, `Paimon`): a rerun of such a query skips the Parquet row groups which held no row of the result in the same snapshot. [#124808](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124808) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The query condition cache now also works for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` queries over Parquet files in `S3` and `AzureBlobStorage`: a rerun of such a query over the same objects skips the row groups which held no row of the result. [#124813](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124813) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The pipeline executor no longer allocates memory for its work queues after executing every task, which reduces the overhead of processing a block. [#124818](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124818) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `ORDER BY <String column> LIMIT n` over `Parquet` files skips row groups by the min/max statistics of the sort column, as it already did for numeric keys, without the per-row filter that `use_top_k_dynamic_filtering_for_variable_length_types` controls: `SELECT Title FROM file('hits.parquet') ORDER BY Title DESC LIMIT 10` skips 203 of its 226 row groups and is about 10 times faster at 4 threads. Closes [#124560](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124560). [#124827](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124827) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `ORDER BY ... LIMIT n` with the dynamic top-K filter on an integer, date/time, decimal or string column and `n` plus `OFFSET` up to 1000 now keeps one threshold for all sorting streams instead of one per stream, so the threshold no longer gets looser with more threads, and more Parquet row groups and MergeTree granules are skipped. `SELECT WatchID, URL FROM hits ORDER BY WatchID LIMIT 10` over ClickBench `hits.parquet` with `max_threads = 16` skips 200 of its row groups instead of about 100 and runs about 2x faster. [#124829](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124829) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A Parquet `INT64` column without a logical type that is read as `DateTime` (like `EventTime` in ClickBench's `hits.parquet`) now uses its min/max statistics, so `WHERE` conditions and `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` skip row groups. [#124863](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124863) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The query condition cache now also works for Parquet files read through the `URL` table engine: a rerun of a query with the same filter skips the row groups without matching rows, for files modified more than a minute ago. [#124874](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124874) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
