* Adaptive codec selection tries every codec on one block in `adaptive_codec_selection_period` (default 8) and reuses the winner on the rest until it stops winning. Merges with `enable_adaptive_codec_selection` now cost about the same as without it. [#124882](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124882) ([Raufs Dunamalijevs](https://github.com/rienath)).
* Sets of `IN` with a subquery spill to disk by default: `max_bytes_ratio_before_external_set` now defaults to `0.5`, so a set spills once query memory exceeds half of the memory that the server or user memory limits leave. Set it to `0` to keep such sets in memory. Sets on disk are built and looked up several times faster: they keep numbers, dates, short `FixedString` values and tuples of them in order, pack close keys, read neighboring blocks together without decompression, skip blocks with Bloom filters, cache the blocks that lookups read, and look up each distinct key of a block of rows once. The new settings `max_bytes_for_external_set_bloom_filter` and `max_bytes_for_external_set_cache`, 128 MiB each by default, limit the memory of the Bloom filters and of the cache. [#124907](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124907) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Async inserts with deduplication no longer wait for a deduplication cache refresh when no duplicate was missed. Before, such an insert could wait up to `deduplication_hashes_cache_update_wait_ms` for each partition after the first one, for each Keeper retry, and after `REPLACE PARTITION`. [#124944](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124944) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).
* Avoid rewriting non-packed compact parts during MATERIALIZE TTL when materialize_ttl_recalculate_only = 1. [#125025](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125025) ([Seva Potapov](https://github.com/seva-potapov)).
* Speeds up `CRC64` and `CRC32IEEE` on long strings by processing eight bytes per step. [#125067](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125067) ([Eric Kurbanov](https://github.com/mrrandom0110)).
* `ORDER BY <column> LIMIT n` on a `MergeTree` table now uses the top-K dynamic filter when the only sort column is a `String` (also inside `Nullable`), controlled by the new setting `use_top_k_dynamic_filtering_for_single_string_sort_column` (enabled by default). When most rows of a block are equal to the threshold, as in a column of mostly empty strings, the filter also skips those rows. For example, ClickBench `WHERE SearchPhrase <> '' ORDER BY SearchPhrase LIMIT 10` repeated with the query condition cache is about 12 times faster. [#125122](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125122) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster index analysis for filters like `toUnixTimestamp(t) >= C` or `toUInt32(t) < C` on a `DateTime` column: the conversion is no longer executed on every granule's bounds. [#125163](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125163) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* `ALTER TABLE ... EXECUTE remove_orphan_files` and `ALTER TABLE ... EXECUTE expire_snapshots` on Iceberg tables no longer re-read every manifest file for each snapshot that lists it, so the number of manifest files they read grows with the number of distinct manifests instead of quadratically with the number of snapshots. Closes [#125347](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125347). [#125402](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125402) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up `bitmapContains` with a constant bitmap by caching the container of the previous lookup across rows. [#125451](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125451) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* An equality with a `Bool` value that no statistics can estimate, such as `WHERE j.f = false` on a JSON path, a `Bool` column without statistics, or `IN` with a `Bool` set, is now estimated to match half of the rows per value instead of 1%, so a join on such a filtered table builds its hash table from the smaller side again. [#125467](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125467) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A filtered read of a glob of local Parquet files (`File` engine, `file()` table function) no longer opens the files whose row groups the min/max statistics already excluded: an earlier read keeps those statistics with the cached file metadata, so later queries skip such files even with other constants. [#125528](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125528) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `LEFT JOIN` queries that return a right-side key column now insert runs of unmatched (or matched) rows at once and the remaining rows without virtual calls, and `Nullable` columns insert default values in bulk. A `LEFT JOIN` on `Nullable` keys against a small table that returns all columns is up to 1.5 times faster. [#125542](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125542) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Function `stringBytesUniq` is up to 2.3 times faster on long strings. [#125680](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125680) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improve parallel replica execution for queries that aggregate over subqueries with redundant ORDER BY clauses by allowing replicas to perform the aggregation. Also enable automatic parallel replica plan matching for these queries. [#125685](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125685) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Reading a single large local Parquet file through the `file` table function or the `File` table engine is now parallelized across all threads: the file is split into buckets of row groups, each read by its own source, with the file metadata parsed only once. On ClickBench, reading a single `hits.parquet` file is now as fast as reading the same data split into 100 files, and up to 3x faster than before on the heaviest queries. [#125738](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125738) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Backported in [#133423](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133423): `GROUP BY ... LIMIT` queries over parallel replicas now prune the aggregation on every replica with the top-K heap (`enable_group_by_top_k_optimization`), with or without `ORDER BY` by the keys, and automatic parallel replicas decides after the plan is optimized, so it compares the plan that would actually run. [#125888](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125888) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* A two-level `GROUP BY` that holds few keys no longer enables software prefetch only because its preallocated buckets are larger than the L2 cache. Such queries were up to 3x slower since 26.7, for example 13 keys with `group_by_two_level_threshold_bytes = 1`. Closes [#125845](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125845). [#125897](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125897) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster reading of Parquet columns with `DELTA_BINARY_PACKED` encoding (e.g. timestamps written by pyarrow or arrow-rs). [#125961](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125961) ([starpact](https://github.com/starpact)).
* Hash joins now store `String` and `Nullable(String)` columns of the right table in the row store when their average value size (from `basic` column statistics) is at most 15 bytes. Joins that output several such columns from a large right table can achieve speedups of 1.4x to 2.4x or higher with more columns, with about 9% to 15% more memory for the right table. Controlled by `min_string_column_bytes_for_hash_join_row_store` (default 8 MiB, `0` disables). [#125979](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125979) ([Hechem Selmi](https://github.com/m-selmi)).
* Faster formatting of `Float32` values (about 1.25x) and of small `Float64` values (up to 2x) when converting to text, by updating the `zmij` library. [#126007](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126007) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* A hash join that can spill to disk, which is the default since `max_bytes_ratio_before_external_join` is `0.5`, no longer passes every joined block through the shared processors of its spilling stage, and skips that stage when it did not spill. This removes most of the overhead such a join had compared to `max_bytes_ratio_before_external_join = 0`. [#126034](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126034) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Estimating subcolumn sizes for `PREWHERE` (for example `m['key']` of a `Map(LowCardinality(String), String)` column) no longer reads the `LowCardinality` dictionary or the `Variant` discriminators header of every data part, which made planning slow on object storage, especially for each new map key. Closes [#114598](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/114598). [#126046](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126046) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading subcolumns (for example, typed paths of a `JSON` column) from MergeTree tables with many parts is faster: a reader now takes the serialization of a static subcolumn from the data part instead of resolving it again every time it is created. [#126077](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126077) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* `GROUP BY` and `DISTINCT` over several fixed-width keys, some of them `Nullable`, are faster: the keys of a block are packed at once instead of row by row. Such queries take 12-26% less time on typical nullable key sets and up to 2.5x less with few distinct groups. Closes [#120100](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120100). [#126098](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126098) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `BACKUP` uses less memory for backups of many files, especially incremental ones: the backup no longer keeps a finished thread pool task for every file it backs up. [#126130](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126130) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* The row estimate no longer treats an always-true `x = x` condition (one non-`Nullable` column of an integer, `Decimal`, `Enum`, date or time, string, `UUID` or IP type) as selective. A correlated subquery that correlates by equality keeps such a condition for each correlation key, so a multi-key `EXISTS` estimated its inner table several times too small and could get a single-threaded hash join. Closes [#122677](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122677). [#126131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126131) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With parallel replicas and `use_skip_indexes_on_data_read` (the default), follower replicas now evaluate skip indexes only over the primary key ranges of each part instead of the whole part, so they read about as much skip index data as the initiator. Closes [#124628](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/124628). [#126145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126145) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Hash joins that output no column of the right table, for example `SELECT count() FROM t LEFT JOIN r ON t.x = r.y`, no longer call a function for every matched row, which makes their probe loop up to about 20% faster. [#126173](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126173) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A window function that is referenced several times, for example through a chain of aliases or in both the `SELECT` list and `QUALIFY`, is now computed once instead of once per reference. Such queries became several times slower with the analyzer compared to the old query analysis. [#126209](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126209) ([Yakov Olkhovskiy](https://github.com/yakov-olkhovskiy)).
* `groupConcat` of `String` values is several times faster and uses less memory. [#126244](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126244) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* `toIPv4`, `toIPv4OrZero` and `toIPv4OrNull` from `String` are now up to 2.5x times faster for canonical addresses. [#126247](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126247) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Faster inserts with `fsync_after_insert`: when the table is on local disks, an `INSERT` writing many parts no longer waits for the fsync after every part. Instead, the files of all its parts are fsynced together before the `INSERT` is acknowledged. This is controlled by the new `MergeTree` setting `group_fsync_before_insert_finish`, enabled by default for non-replicated tables. [#126251](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126251) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A `Memory` table with `compress = true` decompresses only the parts of a column that a subcolumn consists of: `size` of a `String` (used by `length`, `empty` and `notEmpty`), `size0` of an `Array`, `null` of a `Nullable`, tuple elements, and `keys`, `values` and `size0` of a `Map`. A query that reads a column together with its subcolumns decompresses the column once instead of once per name. [#126253](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126253) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster filtering of sparse columns: sorted index lookups continue from the previous match. [#126274](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126274) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* Blocks of `bloom_filter` skip indices that do not compress well are now stored uncompressed, so index analysis does not spend time decompressing them. [#126310](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126310) ([Anton Popov](https://github.com/CurtizJ)).
* Reading Parquet from S3, URL, Azure or HDFS now benefits from a larger `max_download_threads` for column chunks without an offset index: the column data of upcoming row groups is prefetched while earlier ones are decoded, within a memory budget that follows `max_memory_usage` and the user and server limits. On a 20 ms RTT endpoint, ClickBench queries over 100 Parquet objects run up to 2.4x faster at `max_download_threads = 16`. [#126368](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126368) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `GROUP BY` whose groups are capped, by the trivial `GROUP BY ... LIMIT` optimization or by `max_rows_to_group_by` with `group_by_overflow_mode` `'any'` or `'break'`, now keeps its own hash table size statistics, and so does an aggregation over such a capped one. It no longer preallocates the hash table of the same aggregation without the cap, and the next uncapped run still preallocates for all its groups. Closes [#122608](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/122608). [#126374](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126374) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `BACKUP` uses less memory per file it backs up: an entry of the backup no longer keeps a copy of the description of the disk the file is on. A backup of a table with millions of files saves a few gigabytes. [#126388](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126388) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* `BACKUP` no longer builds a lookup table over the names of all the files it backs up when nothing needs it. A backup of a table with millions of files saves about a gigabyte. [#126407](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126407) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* Join reordering estimates a relation with `arrayJoin` or `ARRAY JOIN` as 100 rows per input row instead of leaving its size unknown, so a hash join builds its hash table from a small or empty array join input instead of reading the other input into it. Closes [#126481](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/126481). [#126491](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126491) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading an `Arrow` file from a pipe or a compressed file now needs about one copy of the file in memory instead of 2.25–4.5 times its size. [#126520](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126520) ([Vitaly Pryakhin](https://github.com/naushniki)).
* Faster growth of hash tables: the rehashing loop no longer reloads table state or compares keys for each element. [#126522](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126522) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* `hasAny` and `hasAll` with a long constant array of numbers are much faster: the elements are looked up in a table of the constant instead of searching it for each element. [#126529](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126529) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Fix cubic complexity of enumerating subcolumns of deeply nested `Array` types. [#126554](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126554) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Functions of two geometries, such as `polygonsWithinCartesian`, no longer convert a constant argument for every row. This takes much less memory and time when a large constant polygon is compared with many rows. [#126602](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126602) ([sz-kristof](https://github.com/sz-kristof)).
* The statistics of data parts are loaded in parallel (up to `max_threads` threads) for part pruning by statistics, the ordering of `PREWHERE` conditions and `min`/`max`/`count` from statistics. This speeds up the first queries over tables with many parts on a cold disk. [#126625](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126625) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Column statistics are no longer loaded for pruning parts and ordering `PREWHERE` conditions when the primary key leaves fewer than `min_marks_per_part_to_load_statistics` (64 by default) marks to read per part whose statistics would be loaded, which speeds up queries with a selective primary key condition on a cold start. [#126627](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126627) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Merging large `uniqCombined`, `uniqCombined64` and `uniqHLL12` states is faster, and finalizing them on ARM no longer uses software 128-bit `long double` arithmetic. Results and serialized states do not change. [#126737](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126737) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The streaming exchange of a distributed query plan compresses with `LZ4` by default instead of the codec of `network_compression_method`. New settings `distributed_plan_exchange_compression_method` and `distributed_plan_exchange_zstd_compression_level` choose the codec. [#126743](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126743) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* Faster startup of the macOS arm64 binary: it is now linked with chained fixups, which makes `clickhouse local` start about 4-6 ms faster and use about 11-14 MiB less memory. [#126753](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126753) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Removed about 700 static initializers that ran at the start of every ClickHouse program, including `clickhouse --version`. [#126791](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126791) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* `parallel_replicas_min_number_of_rows_per_replica` is ignored while `automatic_parallel_replicas_mode` builds its plan with parallel replicas, including in subqueries and views. Previously the row estimate for it ran a full extra index analysis on every such build, and its threshold could keep that plan from using replicas. [#126815](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126815) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* `ORDER BY ... COLLATE` is up to 3 times faster: strings are now compared with ICU's UTF-8 collation API instead of a character iterator. Comparing a string of 2 GiB or more under a collation now throws `TOO_LARGE_STRING_SIZE` instead of returning a wrong order. [#126829](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126829) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `LEFT ARRAY JOIN` is about 20% faster on long `Array(String)` columns: each window of the joined arrays is now copied once instead of up to three times. The same applies to `arrayJoin(emptyArrayToSingle(x))`, which is executed as `LEFT ARRAY JOIN`. [#126838](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126838) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Define estimated keys count in iceberg. [#126923](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126923) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* With parallel replicas and `use_skip_indexes_on_data_read`, follower replicas now evaluate skip indexes over the mark ranges analyzed on the initiator, received from the coordinator, instead of repeating the primary key analysis. [#126954](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126954) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* The `SLRU` filesystem cache policy now moves a partially downloaded file segment to the protected queue on a cache hit, as it does for a fully downloaded one. Before, a scan could evict the data of narrow reads from the probationary queue. [#126963](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126963) ([Sema Checherinda](https://github.com/CheSema)).
* Reading a Parquet file with a filter like `x != c` or `x NOT IN (...)` no longer hashes the dictionary page or reads the bloom filter of every row group: for such a condition neither can skip a row group. A scan with `WHERE s != ''` over a dictionary-encoded column is up to 1.4x faster. [#126970](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126970) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `hasAny` over `Array(String)` with a constant array of strings is faster when that array has fewer than four strings, contains a string longer than 256 bytes, or the block is too small for the hash lookup: the elements are now compared with the strings directly instead of through a virtual call per comparison. [#126975](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126975) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Plan-based parallel replicas (`parallel_replicas_plan_based = 1`) used through a `Distributed` table now apply primary key and skip index analysis on the followers instead of reading whole parts. [#127014](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127014) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* The replicas of a catalog-less Iceberg read through `icebergS3Cluster`, `icebergAzureCluster`, `icebergHDFSCluster` or `icebergLocalCluster` (or `icebergS3` with `parallel_replicas_for_cluster_engines`) no longer list the table's `metadata` directory to find the latest `metadata.json`: the initiator passes them the metadata file of the snapshot it reads, so they send no `ListObjects` requests for it and read the same snapshot as the initiator. Closes [#127060](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127060). [#127085](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127085) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* New setting `iceberg_list_only_v_prefixed_metadata_files` (disabled by default): a query on an Iceberg table read by path (without a catalog) on S3 or Azure then finds the latest `metadata.json` by listing only the key prefix `metadata/v` instead of the whole `metadata` directory with every manifest, when the table names its metadata files `v<N>...` (as ClickHouse and Hadoop catalogs do). Enable it only for such tables: in a directory that also holds `<N>-<uuid>.metadata.json` files, those are not candidates. Closes [#127051](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127051). [#127094](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127094) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading an Iceberg table no longer makes an object metadata request (S3 `HeadObject`, Azure `GetProperties`) for every manifest file and, when it is read through a table, for every Parquet or ORC data file. The data file sizes come from the table metadata, and the filesystem, page, Parquet metadata and columns caches keep working without an ETag. A table with 1,007 data files and 671 manifests made 1,678 such requests per query. [#127099](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127099) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Iceberg manifest files are now read ahead on the IO thread pool, up to 64 at a time, and parsed by the threads that read the table, so a query over a table with many manifests on object storage overlaps more round trips (3.2x faster on 200 manifests with 400 ms per read). The thread pool for decoding manifests is removed, and the settings `iceberg_manifest_decode_concurrency` (and its old name `iceberg_delete_manifest_decode_concurrency`), `iceberg_file_entries_queue_size`, `max_iceberg_manifest_decode_thread_pool_size`, `max_iceberg_manifest_decode_thread_pool_free_size` and `iceberg_manifest_decode_thread_pool_queue_size` are obsolete and have no effect. [#127100](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127100) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The columns cache for `Parquet` files no longer copies the columns it writes or serves: a row group is read in chunks of 65536 rows, and a whole chunk is shared between the cache and the query. A query that fills the cache got about 6% faster and uses less memory, and repeated queries are faster too. [#127131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127131) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Update `zstd` to the latest upstream development version with pending performance improvements. Decompression of `ZSTD(3)` data is up to 20% faster on x86. [#127206](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127206) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Queries over `system.symbols` that do not read the XRay columns `symbol_demangled` and `function_id` no longer demangle every symbol of the binary, in builds with XRay enabled. [#127211](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127211) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* `T64` decompression no longer depends on where the stack places its bit matrix: the matrix is aligned to its size and filled with masked loads on AVX2, up to 25% faster for sequential and monotonic data. [#127212](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127212) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* The per-row transform of the `add*`/`subtract*` interval functions over `DateTime64` and `Time64` is always inlined and uses a single inline division instead of `std::div`, so it no longer depends on inliner heuristics that varied between builds. [#127213](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127213) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* `h3CellAreaM2`, `h3CellAreaRads2`, `geoToUTM`, `UTMToGeo` and `colorOKLCHToSRGB` compute the sine and cosine of one angle with a single `sincos` call in every build, with identical results. [#127214](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127214) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Speed up the `sipHash128` function on strings by about 10% for strings of varying length. [#127224](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127224) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Speed up `CAST` from `Enum8` and `Enum16` to `LowCardinality(String)`, also inside `Map`, `Array` and `Tuple`. [#127241](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127241) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* Hash joins now store `Tuple` and `Nullable(Tuple)` columns of fixed-width elements in the row-major hash table payload. Joins that output such columns run up to 1.5 times faster, especially for wide tuples and tuples with `Nullable` elements. [#127273](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127273) ([Hechem Selmi](https://github.com/m-selmi)).
* Slightly faster reading of `String` columns stored with a separate size stream (the default): up to 3% for columns of short strings. [#127277](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127277) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* The radix partitioned `uniqExact` (and `count(DISTINCT)`) now supports arguments of every type, including `Decimal`, `DateTime64`, big integers, `FixedString`, `Tuple`, `Array`, `Map` and `LowCardinality`. It is 2-20x faster than before for these types, and faster for short strings. [#127280](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127280) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Fixed slow inserts and merges on encrypted object storage disks: the files of a part were uploaded one at a time instead of in parallel, which made tables with many columns or `JSON` paths very slow. [#127296](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127296) ([Shaohua Wang](https://github.com/tiandiwonder)).
* `predictXGBoost` now runs in parallel on all query threads instead of using a single CPU core. The `verbosity` parameter of the `XGBOOST` dictionary layout no longer accepts the debug level `3`. [#127328](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127328) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* With the experimental columns cache enabled, conditions on all queried columns (such as `WHERE URL LIKE '%google%'` in `SELECT count()`) are again moved to `PREWHERE` and filtered during the scan, so that queries served from the cache are up to 5 times faster. [#127332](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127332) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* In distributed query plans (`make_distributed_plan`), the stages that feed the build side of a hash join now start before the stages that feed its probe side, so the build side no longer waits behind the dispatch of the probe-side tasks. [#127423](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127423) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* Queries that read `Parquet` columns from the columns cache after `PREWHERE` are faster: the rows that passed are collected 64 at a time instead of one by one (ClickBench Q31 with its columns cached takes 30% less time). [#127475](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127475) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Bump jemalloc for the fiber-safe TLS rework (upstream solution). [#127478](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127478) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Faster primary and partition key analysis for conditions on a monotonic function of a key column, for example `toDate(t)` on a table ordered by `t`: the analysis no longer formats a string for every range it checks. The index analysis of the `long_primary_key` performance test is about two times faster on x86. [#127495](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127495) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster reading of LZ4-compressed MergeTree columns from many small parts: a new read stream of a column starts from the LZ4 decoder statistics of the earlier streams of the same column instead of trying every decoder variant again. ClickBench queries 21, 22, 23 and 26 on a table of about 200 parts are 7-12% faster at 4 threads. [#127536](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127536) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Add the `RabbitMQ` table setting `rabbitmq_max_idle_producer_connections`, which keeps idle connections with their channels open and reuses them across `INSERT` queries. This removes the connection setup from every insert, which is especially costly for materialized views that fire often, including when they write no rows. The default `0` keeps the previous behavior. [#127611](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127611) ([David Mischke](https://github.com/davimi)).
* Merging large `uniqExact` states is several times faster with the new Robin Hood hash tables: the destination set now grows before the merge, so its inserts no longer pile up at the higher load factor. This affects `uniqExactMerge`, merges of `AggregatingMergeTree` parts with `uniqExact` states, and `COUNT(DISTINCT)` together with other aggregates on few threads. [#127625](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127625) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Automatic parallel replicas (`automatic_parallel_replicas_mode`) now collects statistics for queries that join an aggregated subquery whose filter goes to PREWHERE. Previously it could not match the two plans for such queries and built the plan with parallel replicas on every execution without ever using it. [#127628](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127628) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Large inserts into `MergeTree` tables now write their parts with `ZSTD(3)` right away instead of writing them with `LZ4` first, because the size-aware default codec is now evaluated with the uncompressed size of the inserted block. This reduces the amount of data written during a bulk load. [#127652](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127652) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Repeating arrays of numbers, as `materialize` of a constant array and `arrayWithConstant` do, is faster: the result is allocated once, and arrays of up to 64 bytes are copied inline instead of through a separate `memcpy` call. For example, `SELECT has(materialize(range(10)), 5) FROM numbers(10000000)` is about 35% faster. [#127665](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127665) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Queries that use lazy materialization over `MergeTree` (`SELECT * ... ORDER BY ... LIMIT n`) keep all their reading threads busy while the remaining columns are read. Before, a reading thread could wait at random until all threads before it had finished, which made that part of the query up to about twice as long. [#127716](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127716) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Integer division of signed 256-bit integers (for example `intDiv` on `Int256` values) is faster when both operands fit in 64 bits. [#127761](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127761) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading the manifest files of an Iceberg table is faster: fields of a manifest entry are now taken directly from the parsed record instead of enumerating the whole manifest schema for every field. Listing the files of a table with many manifests that are not in the metadata cache is about 3 times faster. [#127782](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127782) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Merges with `merge_use_batch_sorting_queue` enabled no longer fall back to row-by-row insertion for tables with `LowCardinality` columns, which made them slower than with the setting disabled. With the setting enabled, merged parts can be split into blocks differently than on previous versions. The data is the same, but in a `ReplicatedMergeTree` cluster with replicas on different versions this can show up as a harmless "not byte-identical" checksum mismatch, after which the replica downloads the merged part from another replica. [#127826](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127826) ([Christoph Wurm](https://github.com/cwurm)).
* The self-extracting `clickhouse` binary is compressed in parallel, which speeds up builds with `ENABLE_CLICKHOUSE_SELF_EXTRACTING`. The output is unchanged. [#127842](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127842) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* A `TimeSeries` selector no longer repeats a time series once per unmerged part of the time ranges table. [#127847](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127847) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Parsing row-based input formats and `Native` into tables with nested `Tuple` columns is faster for small blocks (streaming engines, async inserts): the per-block columns are now created once per nested element instead of once per nesting level. Closes [#127427](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127427). [#127859](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127859) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reads from the columns cache (`use_columns_cache = 1`) with a `PREWHERE` that skips granules no longer look the cache up again after every skipped part of the range. This made long in-order and `FINAL` reads of cached parts several times slower than reads without the cache. [#127894](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127894) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Without `GROUP BY`, `uniq`, `uniqExact`, `count(DISTINCT)`, `uniqHLL12`, `uniqTheta`, `min`, `max` and `groupUniqArray` of a `LowCardinality(String)` or `LowCardinality(FixedString)` column are faster when values repeat: each distinct value of a block is added once instead of every row (up to 14x faster for columns with few distinct values). [#127940](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127940) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The hash tables of large hash `JOIN`s no longer switch to the Robin Hood mode by default (it was enabled in 26.10). This makes joins with random keys up to 1.8 times faster, but the hash table takes up to 1.75 times more memory and joins on dense keys can be up to 1.3 times slower. The new setting `enable_robin_hood_hash_for_joins` turns the mode back on. [#128012](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128012) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `BACKUP` uses less memory: the name of each file being backed up is no longer kept twice while the backup runs, and a `BACKUP ON CLUSTER` no longer keeps the information about all of its files while writing that same information to ClickHouse Keeper. A backup of a table with millions of files saves gigabytes. [#128020](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128020) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* Faster lookup of names for `Enum16` types with more than 1024 values, such as the `metrics` keys of `system.metric_log`: `CAST` to `String`, text output and casts from numbers are several times faster. [#128127](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128127) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* `CAST` from `Enum8` and `Enum16` to `String` is about 20% faster. [#128134](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128134) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* Iceberg reads skip data files that cannot match a JOIN runtime filter. Controlled by the new setting `use_join_runtime_filters_for_iceberg_pruning`, enabled by default. [#128145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128145) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY` on a single `LowCardinality` key with `count()` as the only aggregate keeps the count in the hash table and looks each dictionary value up once per block, which makes such queries up to 2.5 times faster. [#128149](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128149) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY` on a non-nullable `LowCardinality` key with `ORDER BY count() DESC LIMIT n` (also `uniqExact`, or `count()` next to other aggregates) converts only the best `n` groups of each bucket into the result, like other non-nullable keys. Up to 1.7 times faster, with 8-11% lower peak memory, when most groups are distinct. [#128167](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128167) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Small `MergeTree` reads, such as a repeated `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` query narrowed by the query condition cache to a few dozen granules, now use more of the `max_threads` streams (when `max_threads` is at most 16), with smaller read tasks. Hot ClickBench Q24 and Q26 are 13-14% faster with 4 threads. [#128178](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128178) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `uniqExact` and `COUNT(DISTINCT)` with `GROUP BY` are faster on data where consecutive rows often have the same group and value, as in data sorted by these columns: such a row is no longer inserted into the set again. ClickBench Q9 and Q10 are 1.16x and 1.14x faster with 4 threads. [#128190](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128190) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* JOIN runtime filters on `Nullable` integer keys (for example, optional columns of Iceberg and Parquet tables) look up plain values instead of keys packed with a NULL flag, which makes such joins faster. [#128192](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128192) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster reading of dictionary-encoded, `Nullable` and nested columns from Parquet: bit-packed dictionary indexes and definition/repetition levels are now unpacked by a loop specialized for each bit width. ClickBench queries over `hits.parquet` such as `SELECT AVG(UserID) FROM hits` are 8-15% faster. [#128262](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128262) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster reading of dictionary-encoded columns from Parquet files without a page index when `WHERE` or `PREWHERE` keeps a scattered part of the rows: the row filter is converted to a bitmask once instead of being scanned again for every run of dictionary indexes. Some ClickBench queries over `hits.parquet` are up to 10% faster. [#128287](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128287) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `ORDER BY <String column> LIMIT n` over local `Parquet` files with a filter that rejects the empty string (such as `WHERE s != ''`) now skips row groups by the values of their dictionary when their min/max statistics (minimum `''`) cannot: ClickBench Q26 over its 100 Parquet files is about 11% faster with 4 threads. Closes [#128233](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/128233). [#128290](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128290) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster text output (`toString`, `CAST` to `String`, `JSONEachRow`, `Vertical` and other text formats) of `JSON` paths stored in shared data, i.e. paths beyond `max_dynamic_paths`. Query 1 of the `json_to_string` performance test is about 3 times faster. [#128339](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128339) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster `PREWHERE` over a dictionary-encoded `String` or `FixedString` column of a Parquet file when the condition cannot throw, for example `s != ''`: the dictionary indexes of each block are no longer scanned and renumbered first. ClickBench Q11 and Q12 over Parquet are about 15% faster. [#128342](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128342) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* New `DataLakeCatalog` database setting `snapshot_loading_mode` for Iceberg REST based catalogs. With `'refs'`, ClickHouse asks the catalog to return only the snapshots referenced by branches and tags when it loads a table, which makes queries on tables with a long snapshot history faster. By default the mode that the catalog advertises in its configuration is used (Databricks Unity Catalog advertises `refs`), and `all` if it advertises none. Closes [#128348](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/128348). [#128354](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128354) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Resolving an aggregate function during query analysis no longer copies its registry entry, including its documentation, so queries with many aggregate functions are planned faster: `EXPLAIN PLAN` of ClickBench Q30 (90 `SUM` terms) executes 4.4% fewer instructions and about 2% fewer CPU cycles. [#128359](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128359) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster planning of queries with many `sum`, `count` or `avg` over the same column, such as ClickBench Q30: `optimize_syntax_fuse_functions` now builds the replacement of each function once and copies it to the other equal aggregates. `EXPLAIN PLAN` of Q30 executes 3.9% fewer instructions and 4.3% fewer CPU cycles. [#128371](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128371) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reduced cross-thread contention during primary key index analysis. [#128375](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128375) ([Michael Jarrett](https://github.com/EmeraldShift)).
* Faster filtering of sparse columns that have a few percent of non-default values, for example in `PREWHERE`. [#128396](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128396) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With parallel replicas for object storage and `*Cluster` table functions, an idle replica no longer takes the files assigned to another replica that is still processing its own files, unless that replica has much more work left than the others. This keeps the assignment of files to replicas stable across queries and improves the hit rate of the filesystem and page caches. [#128433](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128433) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A `PREWHERE` split into several read steps no longer filters again, at each step, the columns that only earlier steps used (their inputs and the results of their conditions). ClickBench Q42 and Q43 are 12-14% faster. [#128452](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128452) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) uses less memory for `GROUP BY` with many distinct keys: it stages smaller records in smaller chunks. Across 11 ClickBench aggregation queries, peak memory is 25% lower at 64 threads and 21% lower at 16 threads, at the same speed. A `GROUP BY` without aggregate functions now deduplicates the repeats of a key whenever it can, which makes such queries up to 2.2x faster. [#128495](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128495) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Row estimates no longer treat a filter on a constant column of `UNION ALL` branches (for example `materialize('s') = 's'` after filter push-down) as selective: a comparison or `IS [NOT] NULL` of constants is now estimated as passing every row or none. Join reordering then picks the smaller build side: the hash tables of TPC-DS q4, q11 and q74 hold 7-16 times fewer rows (scale factors 1 and 10). Closes [#128482](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/128482). [#128506](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128506) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `dpsub` join order algorithm is now aware of the sort order of the joined tables: for each inner equi-join it chooses between a hash join and `full_sorting_merge` by cost, and keeps join results sorted by a join key when a later join can use that order. Joins of large `MergeTree` tables on their sorting key use much less memory and run faster. Controlled by the new setting `query_plan_join_order_use_interesting_orders`, enabled by default. [#128519](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128519) ([Fisnik Kastrati](https://github.com/fkastrati)).
* Faster primary key filtering for `IN` conditions on a single integer key column, mostly with small index granules. [#128520](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128520) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Faster `PREWHERE` with several conditions: the block is filtered once instead of after every condition when the later conditions cannot throw. ClickBench Q38 is up to 10% faster. [#128582](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128582) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Skip `tokens_map` probes for tokens outside the keep set using a small register-blocked bloom filter, built for keep sets of at least 64 tokens. [#128609](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128609) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Faster merges of tables with `TTL ... GROUP BY`: columns are now resolved once per block instead of once per row. [#128651](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128651) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Enable `query_plan_top_k_through_array_join` by default. A query with `ARRAY JOIN` and `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` on columns that are not joined now sorts and limits the rows before `ARRAY JOIN`, which uses less memory. [#128705](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128705) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Improved performance of parsing JSON objects into the `JSON` type when fields are stored in shared data, especially for objects with large arrays of sub-objects. Added setting `type_json_skip_duplicate_key_detection` to skip per-object duplicate-key detection when keys are known to have no duplicates. [#128739](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128739) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* Make `isValidASCII` independent of how the compiler autovectorizes the byte reduction. [#128851](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128851) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Slightly faster `index` of sparse columns. [#128852](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128852) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Slightly faster `multiIf` with many branches. [#128853](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128853) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Slightly faster `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` on columns with many equal values. [#128854](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128854) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* Remove a build-dependent slowdown of text index `LIKE` queries. [#128855](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128855) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* The `Kafka`, `RabbitMQ`, `NATS` and `FileLog` table engines read the table name for the `_table` virtual column once per block instead of locking a table-wide mutex for every row, so consumers of one table no longer contend on it. Closes [#120032](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/120032). [#128914](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128914) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The sort that `query_plan_top_k_through_array_join` and `query_plan_top_k_through_join` move now uses the top-K dynamic filter. This lowers memory for `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` over `ARRAY JOIN` with long arrays, and the `JOIN` optimization now goes through all joins of a `LEFT JOIN` chain. [#128926](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128926) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Hash joins on a single `String` key are faster when the right side is large, and their hash tables are smaller. Each key takes 16 bytes in the hash table. A key of up to 15 bytes is stored as it is. A longer key is stored as its 128-bit `SipHash`, with one bit used as a flag. Two different long keys whose other 127 hash bits agree match as equal. Setting `enable_packed_string_keys_in_hash_join` to 0 turns this off. `min_rows_for_packed_string_keys_in_hash_join` (100000 by default) keeps exact keys for a right side estimated at fewer rows. [#128972](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128972) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* Improved the performance of `quantileExact`, `quantileExactLow`, `quantileExactHigh`, `quantileExactExclusive` and `quantileExactInclusive` without `GROUP BY` by adding values a whole block at a time. [#128989](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/128989) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Reduced CPU usage when many threads schedule jobs into a full thread pool, for example while loading many tables at server startup. [#129010](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129010) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* Optimize writing wide packed parts with lots of columns. [#129060](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129060) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Faster conversion of integers to text: signed values of mixed sign, 8-bit values, short values and 128- and 256-bit values. [#129098](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129098) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* `GROUP BY` of fixed-width keys and one `String` key, such as `GROUP BY UserID, minute(EventTime), SearchPhrase`, uses a new hash table. It keeps strings of up to 11 bytes inside the key, or of up to 15 bytes when the fixed-width keys fit in 4 bytes, computes the hash of a key once, and stores keys with an empty string as narrow fixed-width keys. On four ClickBench queries that group by such keys, the geometric mean speedup is 1.19x at 16 threads and 1.13x at 64 threads, up to 1.43x, and peak memory drops by 18% and 12%. The new setting `enable_fixed_and_packed_string_keys_in_aggregation` is enabled by default; disabling it restores the previous hash table. [#129180](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129180) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* JOIN runtime filters on `Int8`, `UInt8`, `Int16` and `UInt16` keys use an exact bitset over all values of the key type instead of a Bloom filter when there are too many keys for the exact set. This removes hashing and false positives from the probe. [#129186](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129186) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `PREWHERE` step over a Parquet file that keeps at least half of the rows now filters the columns already read in place, as MergeTree does, instead of copying them. ClickBench Q28 over its Snappy-compressed Parquet files is about 10% faster. Closes [#129108](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/129108). [#129188](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129188) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `query_plan_propagate_predicate_across_join` now also copies a filter across an equi-join onto a `Parquet` read through `file`, `url` or object storage (not a data lake), when the join key is an integer of up to 64 bits, `Date`, `Date32` or `Decimal` column without a default expression, so the read skips row groups by their min/max statistics. A point lookup joined to a 30M-row Parquet file is about 5 times faster. [#129191](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129191) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Do not probe `data.packed` for every file of a packed part. [#129194](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129194) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* Filtered `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` queries that read a MergeTree table in order with `read_in_order_use_buffering` (the default) now read far fewer rows: an input stops reading ahead once the inputs that sort before it have already produced enough rows for the `LIMIT`, also when the `ORDER BY` only starts with a prefix of the sorting key. The two queries from [#66578](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/66578) read about 4 and 60 times fewer rows. [#129198](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129198) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `sipHash128` is faster on `String` and `FixedString` columns, and on `Nullable`, `LowCardinality`, `Array`, `Map` and `Tuple` of them: it hashes 8 values at a time with NEON on aarch64 and AVX2 on x86. The hashes do not change. [#129204](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129204) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* Faster conversion of `Float64` values to `Int128`, `UInt128`, `Int256`, `UInt256`, `Decimal128` and `Decimal256`. For example, `toDecimal128(x, 6)` of a `Float64` column is about 4x faster on x86-64. [#129274](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129274) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Hash joins whose keys use the `hashed` hash table, such as several columns that do not fit in 32 bytes or a `String` with other columns, are faster: they hash the keys of 8 rows at a time with NEON on aarch64 and AVX2 on x86, and they prefetch the hash table. The keys and the results do not change. [#129328](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129328) ([Harikrishnan Prabakaran](https://github.com/harikrishnan94)).
* An `INSERT` into an Iceberg table on S3 no longer waits for the upload of each finished data file before it writes the next one: up to `iceberg_insert_max_data_files_in_upload` (default 8) finished files of each insert stream are uploaded in the background. A single-stream `INSERT` of 30 million rows into `IcebergS3` is about 3 times faster. [#129428](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129428) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Aggregate window functions computed in several parallel streams, such as `sum(v) OVER (PARTITION BY p ORDER BY i ROWS BETWEEN 1 PRECEDING AND 1 FOLLOWING)`, no longer run up to 2x slower at random: aggregate function states now occupy whole CPU cache lines, so parallel threads stop invalidating each other's cache lines on every row. [#129432](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129432) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improved performance of addition and subtraction over 128-bit and 256-bit integers and decimals, which speeds up `sum`, `sumIf`, `avg`, and the `+` and `-` operators on these types. [#129439](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129439) ([Chiranmoy Bhattacharya](https://github.com/chiranmoyf)).
* `DROP TABLE` of a `KeeperMap` table no longer sends a Keeper `List` request for every key before removing it, and removes the keys of a table with fewer than 1000 keys in the same Keeper request as its metadata when Keeper supports `RemoveRecursive`. Dropping a table with 200000 keys is more than 10 times faster, and so is a server shutdown that waits for such a drop. [#129440](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129440) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improved performance of `sum`, `avg` and `sumWithOverflow` over `Decimal32` and `Decimal64`, including their `-If` variants and `Nullable` arguments, and of other aggregate sums over decimals. [#129441](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129441) ([Chiranmoy Bhattacharya](https://github.com/chiranmoyf)).
* Lazy materialization of `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` queries over Parquet files in object storage, also as the data files of Iceberg tables, now reads the deferred columns of different data files in parallel, with up to `max_threads` readers, as for `MergeTree`. A query that reads many columns lazily uses fewer readers, so that its memory usage does not grow. [#129443](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129443) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `has`, `notHas`, `mapContainsKey`, `mapKeys`, `mapValues`, `mapContainsValue`, `mapContainsKeyLike` and `mapContainsValueLike` over a `Map` column of Parquet files read through `file`, `url`, `s3` and data lake tables such as Iceberg now decode only the keys or only the values of the map when the map is the only column the query reads from the table, with the type exceptions these rewrites have on `MergeTree`. Up to 14x faster in the Iceberg runs of the map perf tests. Closes [#129354](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/129354). [#129462](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129462) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `uniqExact` (and `count(DISTINCT)`) over one `String`, `FixedString` or `IPv6` argument is up to 2.7 times faster with `GROUP BY`. The serialized states do not change. [#129490](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129490) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster decoding and encoding of posting lists with the `pfor` codec: queries over frequent tokens use 10-20% less CPU. [#129496](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129496) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Backported in [#134099](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/134099): Automatic parallel replicas no longer builds a plan with parallel replicas for a query whose correlated subquery rules them out. [#129544](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129544) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`) merges the hash tables of its threads through an index of their cells, instead of converting them to two-level tables (see `group_by_two_level_threshold`) or walking every table for every hash partition (`enable_parallel_single_level_merge`). `GROUP BY` queries whose tables hold 15-16 thousand keys per thread without freezing, with states such as `groupArray`, `uniqExact`, `any` or `groupBitmap`, run 1.34x faster at 64 threads, and ClickBench Q8 and Q9 use 21-25% less memory at 64 threads. The byte bound of the freeze, `adaptive_aggregator_freeze_threshold_bytes`, now counts the hash-table buffer of a table instead of its tracked memory, so tables of few keys with growing states keep aggregating in place. [#129565](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129565) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Lower memory usage of an `INSERT ... SELECT` with `async_insert = 1` whose result has several blocks: the synchronous insert it falls back to now keeps sparse columns instead of converting the whole insert block to full columns (about 6 times less memory for a wide table with sparse columns). [#129574](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129574) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* JOIN runtime filters now skip granules when the join key is the global row index `_part_starting_offset + _part_offset`, so a self-join that fetches the rows found by a subquery reads only the granules of those rows. [#129677](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129677) ([Shankar Iyer](https://github.com/shankar-iyer)).
* Expanding a sparse `String` column to a full one (for `GROUP BY`, `JOIN`, sorting and most functions) now copies its values in one pass, which makes such queries over mostly-empty `String` columns up to 10% faster. [#129692](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129692) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Distributed queries with `make_distributed_plan` no longer stall for seconds when a shuffle opens many exchange connections at once: the streaming exchange server queues as many pending connections as `listen_backlog` instead of 64. [#129701](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129701) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* Faster `WHERE` filtering of a sparse column by a condition that keeps exactly its non-default rows (for example `WHERE s != ''` on a sparse `String` column): the column's values are shared instead of copied. [#129705](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129705) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Iceberg reads skip the Parquet row groups that cannot match a JOIN runtime filter. Controlled by the setting `use_join_runtime_filters_for_iceberg_pruning`. [#129717](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129717) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `hasPhrase` on a text index with positions is up to about 25% faster for phrases that match many rows. [#129727](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129727) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Faster `PREWHERE` filtering of a sparse column by a condition that keeps exactly its non-default rows (for example `PREWHERE s != ''` on a sparse `String` column): the column's values are shared instead of copied. [#129738](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129738) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading sparse columns is faster: the offsets of their non-default values are decoded from a local pointer instead of updating the read buffer position for every byte. [#129768](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129768) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The senders and the receivers of the streaming exchanges of a distributed query plan prepare each column type once, not once per packet. [#129831](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129831) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* Faster squashing of blocks with `JSON` columns, up to 30% in the new performance test: the memory reserved for the dynamic paths of a `JSON` column now includes the rows already in the destination block. [#129846](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129846) ([Christoph Viebig](https://github.com/cv4g)).
* Faster `WHERE` filtering when other sparse columns of the block have almost the same non-default rows as the filter column and the condition is not moved to `PREWHERE`: their consecutive values are copied with one call instead of one call per row. [#129855](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129855) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `WHERE` condition on the only column of `ORDER BY ... LIMIT`, like in `SELECT s FROM t WHERE s != '' ORDER BY s LIMIT 10`, is now moved to `PREWHERE` next to the top-K filter, which reads that column anyway, so the query condition cache remembers the granules the condition rejects. Repeated runs of ClickBench Q25 are about 2x faster. [#129882](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129882) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* On Linux, the `clickhouse` binary now reserves a file descriptor table of `min(4096, soft RLIMIT_NOFILE)` entries before any thread starts, so short `clickhouse local` queries that open many files, such as a query over a directory of Parquet files, no longer wait for the kernel to grow the table. [#129906](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129906) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* On Linux, `clickhouse-local` now uses one jemalloc arena per 8 CPUs (at least two) instead of one arena per CPU, so a one-shot query faults in fewer pages: on 16 CPUs, ClickBench queries over Parquet files take 15% fewer page faults, 9% less peak memory and about 5% to 10% less time (geometric mean). An explicit `MALLOC_CONF` is used as is. [#129930](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129930) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `uniqExact` and `count(DISTINCT)` over several arguments, over one `Tuple`, or over one argument of a type without a specialized implementation (such as `Decimal`) are up to 1.8 times faster. The serialized states do not change. [#129941](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/129941) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY ... ORDER BY <aggregate> LIMIT n` keeps skipping the groups that cannot reach the top when `max_rows_to_group_by` is set with `group_by_overflow_mode = 'throw'`. Such a limit used to turn off the top-K pruning of the adaptive aggregator and the top-K threshold merge, however high it was; now it is checked against the groups that the query builds. On three such queries over the ClickBench `hits` table, with a limit far above their number of groups, the geometric mean speedup is 1.33x at 16 threads and 1.22x at 64 threads, up to 1.47x. [#130077](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130077) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Slightly faster memory deallocation: sized `operator delete` no longer looks up the allocation size in jemalloc. Also fixes memory tracking drift for over-aligned allocations. [#130118](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130118) ([Manuel](https://github.com/raimannma)).
* Faster `DISTINCT`, `INTERSECT`, `EXCEPT` and `IN` when the key is hashed as a whole (several columns with a `String`, a `Tuple`, or fixed-width keys wider than 32 bytes): the keys of up to 256 rows are hashed before they are looked up. Up to 2x faster for `DISTINCT` on one thread. [#130137](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130137) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improve text formatting performance for `Decimal256` values with large fractional parts. [#130145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130145) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Improve division and remainder operations on `UInt256`, `Int256`, and `Decimal256` with single-limb divisors on AArch64 by reusing reciprocal preparation and using shifts for powers of two. [#130161](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130161) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Improve `toValidUTF8` performance on AArch64 by avoiding repeated ASCII vector probes on non-ASCII text and skipping ASCII prefixes and tails more efficiently. [#130162](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130162) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Faster reading of nullable, `Bool` and `Int8` columns from ORC files: their run-length encoded streams are now decoded in bulk instead of one value at a time. [#130200](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130200) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `CREATE TABLE` in a database whose metadata is on a `plain_rewritable` disk over S3 or Azure Blob Storage writes the table metadata file directly, instead of a temporary file that is then copied to the final key and deleted: one request to the object storage per table instead of nine. Closes [#130164](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/130164). [#130224](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130224) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Fixed excessive memory use when writing Parquet files with zstd compression at high compression levels (for example, output_format_compression_level = 22). Pages are now compressed with their size known up front instead of through a streaming compressor, so zstd no longer allocates oversized buffers. In one example query, peak memory dropped from about 6 GB to about 320 MB. [#130231](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130231) ([Armin Braun](https://github.com/original-brownbear)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) runs `GROUP BY` with `ORDER BY count() LIMIT n` faster when `n` is large or the order is ascending, and the same with `uniqExact` and `uniqExactIf`: the threads of its merge share the best counts found so far, drop the groups that cannot reach the top, and stop merging an ascending order once it holds `n` groups with the smallest possible count. On seven such queries over the ClickBench `hits` table, the geometric mean speedup is 1.15x at 16 threads and 1.17x at 64 threads, up to 1.57x, and peak memory drops by up to 16%. [#130281](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130281) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) now turns itself off for later runs of a query only when at least half of the threads of a run switched back to ordinary aggregation. Before, it could turn itself off while most threads kept using it, which slowed down repeated queries such as `GROUP BY` without aggregate functions. On two such queries over the ClickBench `hits` table, the geometric mean speedup of the later runs is 1.32x at 16 threads, up to 1.34x, and the runs at 64 threads are unchanged. [#130306](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130306) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* A `text` index on an `Array` column is now used for `arrayJoin(column) = const` and `arrayJoin(column) [GLOBAL] IN (set)`, also with `transform_null_in = 1`, in the same way as for `has(column, const)` and `hasAny(column, set)`. Before, these conditions read every granule. [#130338](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130338) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Fixed a mutex bottleneck in `IDisk::isCaseInsensitive` that made inserts and merges on object storage disks (`s3_plain_rewritable`, S3, Azure) spend most of their time in system calls when a table has many column substreams, for example a `JSON` column. The cached result is now checked before `isReadOnly`. [#130384](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130384) ([Chez](https://github.com/ryechezkel)).
* Automatic parallel replicas no longer repeats the index analysis of a join's tables when it builds the plan with parallel replicas to decide whether to use them. [#130516](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130516) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* A `UNION ALL` branch or subquery whose filter becomes always false after filter push-down (for example an outer `WHERE tag = 'y'` over branches that select the literal tags `'x'`, `'y'` and `'z'`) no longer builds the sets of its `IN (subquery)` conditions or reads its input. The query from [#130319](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/130319) now peaks at 1.2 GB instead of 4.0 GB and runs 2.3 times faster. Closes [#130319](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/130319). [#130526](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130526) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `FINAL` query on a table whose row policy compares `ORDER BY` columns with a value computed once per query, such as `currentUser()`, `now()` or an `IN (subquery)` set, applies the policy before the merge again, as in 26.8, instead of reading and merging every row first. On `GraphiteMergeTree` this holds only for a policy on the path column when it leads `ORDER BY`. Closes [#130317](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/130317). [#130536](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130536) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Joins with `enable_join_runtime_filters` no longer carry the column a computed join key (for example `ON l.k = r.k + 1`) was derived from into the join as unused payload, which made such joins slower with runtime filters than without. [#130546](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130546) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)).
* A `SQL SECURITY DEFINER` view queried by its own definer is no longer read as an opaque step, so the optimizations of the query apply to it again, as before 26.10 (for example, `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` reads in order through it). Other users, and sessions of the definer that are limited by the `GRANTS` of their authentication method or use other roles, still read such a view sealed, and such sessions of the definer no longer bypass the check that hides its plan in `EXPLAIN`. [#130552](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130552) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Backported in [#133419](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133419): Automatic parallel replicas (`automatic_parallel_replicas_mode = 1`) no longer builds the plan with parallel replicas on every execution of a query whose last build ended without a decision: the execution after the first such build builds it again at once, and from then on one in every `automatic_parallel_replicas_undecided_retry_after_executions + 1` executions (default 10) does. [#130565](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130565) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* A `SELECT ... LIMIT 0` from a MergeTree table no longer runs the primary key and skip index analysis, which took up to seconds on large tables with a filter on the key. Such a query reads nothing, so `max_rows_to_read` and `max_partitions_to_read` no longer reject it. Closes [#125812](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125812). [#130578](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130578) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `LEFT ARRAY JOIN` is faster when no joined array is empty: `emptyArrayToSingle` now returns its argument as is instead of copying it row by row, which makes the `LEFT ARRAY JOIN` query of the `array_join` performance test about 1.6x faster. [#130581](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130581) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SHOW TABLES` and `system.tables` for a `DataLakeCatalog` database over AWS Glue list the Glue databases in parallel, which makes them much faster in accounts with many databases. [#130599](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130599) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Writing Iceberg metadata files with `iceberg_metadata_compression_method = 'zstd'` now sizes the zstd context to the file instead of to the compression level: at `output_format_compression_level = 22` the memory of an `INSERT` or `CREATE TABLE` drops from about 836 MiB to about 2.5 MiB. [#130621](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130621) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Expanding a sparse `FixedString`, `Decimal` or `DateTime64` column to a full one (for `GROUP BY`, `JOIN`, sorting and most functions) now fills the default values in one pass and copies only the non-default ones, which reduces the CPU time of such queries by up to 13%. [#130641](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130641) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Speed up planning of distributed queries with many shards: the initiator plans the query once for all remote shards, not once for each of them (unless `serialize_query_plan` is enabled). [#130655](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130655) ([eoger-ant](https://github.com/eoger-ant)).
* Faster parallel reading of a single local Parquet file whose row groups differ in size: the readers take the next part of the file from a shared queue, largest first, instead of each reading a fixed round-robin share of the row groups. Threads that finish reading memory-mapped files at the same time (e.g. in `clickhouse-local`, which reads files with `mmap` by default) no longer wait for each other to release the mappings. [#130740](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130740) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster reads of MergeTree tables with many columns: preparing the read of each part no longer scans the part's column list once per read column. A `SELECT *` point lookup that reaches 25 parts of a 1600-column table is 30-40% faster. [#130765](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130765) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Iceberg reads now skip the Parquet pages of data files whose page index shows they cannot contain a key of a JOIN runtime filter. Controlled by the setting `use_join_runtime_filters_for_iceberg_pruning`. [#130906](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130906) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster loading of `HASHED` and `SPARSE_HASHED` dictionaries: attribute values are read directly from the source columns instead of through a generic value per row. [#130965](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130965) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Faster `PREWHERE` on low-cardinality `String` columns of Parquet files (up to ~10x when `apply_string_filters_during_scan` is on, the default): the string filter no longer updates its shared statistics for every short run of dictionary-encoded rows. [#130986](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/130986) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* Improve performance of the `encrypt`, `decrypt` and `tryDecrypt` functions in AES-GCM modes by up to 27x with many threads. [#131001](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131001) ([Mikhail Koviazin](https://github.com/mkmkme)).
* `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` over several `ARRAY JOIN` clauses now sorts and limits the rows before all of them, not only before the last one, when the sort key does not use the joined columns. [#131004](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131004) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Faster `randomString`, `boundingRatio`, `array` and `arrayReverse` of complex types, skipping of fields in `TabSeparated`-like formats, the `ODBCDriver2` and `JSONEachRow` output formats, and reading of compact parts with small granules. [#131078](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131078) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
