* Window functions whose sorting reuses the table sorting key (`query_plan_reuse_storage_ordering_for_window_functions`) now run in parallel by `PARTITION BY` instead of in a single stream, and the setting is enabled by default again. [#131135](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131135) ([Nikita Fomichev](https://github.com/fm4v)).
* When a single local Parquet file is read by several streams, the rows read by each stream are now spread over several threads after the read, so a filter that keeps rows from only a few row groups no longer leaves most threads idle. ClickBench Q39 on a single `hits.parquet` is 1.7x faster at 16 and 32 threads. [#131140](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131140) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With the columns cache enabled, reads of `MergeTree` tables with wide rows (for example `JSON`) and small reads use all threads again instead of a few. [#131142](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131142) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` over Parquet files skips row groups by the top-K threshold also when they are served from the columns cache. [#131143](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131143) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Improve numeric array replication and the performance of `arrayWithConstant` and `byteSize` on large arrays of fixed-size values. [#131145](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131145) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Negative `LIMIT BY` (for example `LIMIT -1 BY k`) over data sorted by the key is several times faster when the groups are small. [#131166](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131166) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Iceberg tables keep the row order of a Parquet data file only for files whose position deletes need it, so other files are read with full parallelism and `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` can skip row groups by their statistics: `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` queries over an Iceberg table are up to 2.7 times faster, ClickBench queries 6% faster on average. `ALTER TABLE ... DELETE` and `UPDATE` with `use_roaring_bitmap_iceberg_positional_deletes = 1` no longer write position delete files that make a later read return deleted rows. [#131203](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131203) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A query that combines `x IN (subquery)` with other conditions by `AND` now stops reading as soon as the subquery returns an empty set also when `compile_expressions` has compiled the `AND` (by default once the same expression has been seen `min_count_to_compile_expression` times). Before, such a query, for example with `FINAL`, read the whole key range and only then filtered every row out. [#131211](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131211) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Queries that filter by the key of a normal projection which does not contain all columns of the query are faster with `optimize_use_projection_filtering` (enabled by default): when the projection can only exclude whole parts, its primary-key analysis now stops refining a part once it finds a range that may match. The query from [#131132](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131132) runs about 4 times faster. [#131215](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131215) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A common part of an expression that short-circuit evaluation executes lazily, inside an argument of `and`, `or`, `if` or `multiIf`, is computed once instead of once per use. Filtering on a view column built from `dictGet` and tested in several `OR` branches is up to 4x faster (2x at default settings). Closes [#131136](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131136). [#131227](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131227) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With `use_lightweight_primary_key_index_analysis = 1` (the default), a filter that can never be true on a primary key column that is not kept in memory, such as `c IN (SELECT ...)` over a subquery that returns no rows or only `NULL`, now skips every granule, as it does with the setting at 0, instead of reading the whole table. [#131291](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131291) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading data lakes and object storage with parallel replicas or `*Cluster` table functions no longer serializes file listing and per-file requests on the initiator, so replicas no longer wait for each other. [#131326](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131326) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* A `JOIN` executed with the `ie_join` algorithm (by default, a `JOIN` whose `ON` section has only inequality conditions) no longer slows down quadratically when one side is much larger than the other: a range join of 1 million rows against a 16-row table now takes 0.1 seconds instead of 3.6 seconds. Closes [#131229](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131229). [#131335](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131335) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SELECT ... FINAL` on `ReplacingMergeTree`, `CollapsingMergeTree` and `VersionedCollapsingMergeTree` tables with a skip index and `use_skip_indexes_if_final_exact_mode = 1` reads fewer granules when the partition key is computed from primary key columns: granules that can hold another version of a selected row are now added back only from the same partition. [#131342](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131342) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The server starts about 0.25 s faster: the RSA key pair of the `sha256_password` authentication over the MySQL protocol is generated on first use instead of at startup, where it was generated twice for every MySQL listener. [#131344](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131344) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A `RabbitMQ` table that streams to materialized views no longer keeps a CPU core busy while it waits for messages. [#131355](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131355) ([Sven Eliasson](https://github.com/comino)).
* A condition on a primary key column that cannot use the primary key, such as a join runtime filter on the join key or `key LIKE '%x'`, no longer moves the other primary key conditions out of the end of `PREWHERE`, so the more selective conditions run first. The second query of [#131131](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131131) with `enable_join_runtime_filters = 1` is about 1.4x faster. [#131360](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131360) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Query analysis is faster for queries whose `AND` chains of comparisons read columns of large subqueries, such as many inlined CTEs: `optimize_and_compare_chain` now hashes each operand once instead of on every lookup. Analysis of the query from [#131134](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131134) is 2x faster with the work budget off and 1.5x faster at default settings. [#131361](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131361) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `MergeTree` query that needs no column of the table, such as `SELECT count()` over a table with lightweight-deleted rows, `count()` over a subquery or `SELECT isNullable(c) FROM t`, again reads the column that is smallest on disk, as it does with `query_plan_remove_unused_columns = 0`. Since 25.12 it read the column with the smallest data type, which could be more than 1000 times larger. Closes [#131235](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131235). Closes [#106597](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/106597). [#131371](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131371) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Backported in [#133221](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133221): Read columns of Compact part in the order of the file (to avoid backward seeks). [#131439](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131439) ([Azat Khuzhin](https://github.com/azat)).
* The server starts up to 0.3 s faster with a cold page cache: it builds the symbol index for stack traces in the background instead of before startup. [#131454](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131454) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Use the upstream LZ4 library for native block decompression by default. Set `use_lz4_library_decompression = 0` to restore ClickHouse's adaptive decoder. Existing compressed data remains compatible with both implementations. [#131460](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131460) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Faster reading of a subset of columns from `CustomSeparated` formats with the `Escaped` or `Raw` escaping rule. [#131462](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131462) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Query analysis is faster for a condition written as a deeply nested `AND` tree, such as `((a AND b) AND c) AND ...`, when `optimize_and_compare_chain` is enabled (the default). [#131479](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131479) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) merges the hash tables of a single `FixedString` key, or of a single `String` key with `enable_packed_string_keys_in_aggregation = 0`, through an index of their cells, as it does for other keys, instead of converting them to two-level tables or walking every table for every hash partition. On six queries that group by one such key, the geometric mean speedup is 1.05x at 16 threads and 1.11x at 64 threads, up to 1.23x for a `GROUP BY` of 5 million `FixedString(16)` keys with a top 10 by `count`, and peak memory drops by 1% and 3%. [#131527](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131527) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* A `DISTINCT` over a `FINAL` read of a `ReplacingMergeTree` table no longer reads in the sorting key order when the read would otherwise use vertical `FINAL` (`enable_vertical_final`), unless a `LIMIT` lets it stop early. Reading in order turned vertical `FINAL` off and made such queries up to 2 times slower, also through a join with `query_plan_read_in_order_through_join`. Closes [#131268](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131268). [#131547](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131547) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) now also handles `GROUP BY` of a single `Nullable` key, such as a column of a table created from a CSV or Parquet file with an inferred schema. On five ClickBench queries and one more query over a copy of `hits` whose columns are all `Nullable`, the geometric mean speedup is 1.41x at 16 threads and 1.24x at 64 threads, up to 1.66x, and peak memory drops by 39% and 28%. [#131551](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131551) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* `SELECT ... FINAL WHERE <filter> LIMIT n` on a `ReplacingMergeTree` table with `enable_vertical_final` (the default) now stops reading early when the rows that pass the filter come from a small part, instead of reading the whole table. Closes [#131381](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131381). [#131560](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131560) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A multi-step `PREWHERE` over Compact parts no longer reads the granules that an earlier step filtered out completely again in its later steps and in the final read, whatever the read block size. For the query from [#131382](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131382) this reads 45% fewer compressed bytes and runs up to 1.5x faster. Closes [#131382](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131382). [#131569](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131569) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Query analysis is faster for queries whose `WHERE` or `JOIN ON` is an `OR` of `AND` chains over columns of a large subquery or CTE: `optimize_extract_common_expressions` now hashes each condition once per extraction instead of on every lookup. Analysis of the query from [#131572](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131572) is 1.8x faster at default settings. [#131594](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131594) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Sorting by several columns and merging sorted data, including merges of `MergeTree` tables, is faster when the sorting key contains a `Decimal32`, `Decimal64`, `DateTime64` or `Time64` column: the row comparator for such keys is now JIT-compiled, as it already was for integer and string keys. [#131635](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131635) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Optimize short literal/match sequences in ClickHouse's `LZ4` decoder. Add the disabled-by-default `use_lz4_library_decompression` setting to optionally use the upstream decoder for `LZ4` and `LZ4HC` data without rewriting existing parts. [#131670](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131670) ([Ilya Yatsishin](https://github.com/qoega)).
* Read in order, aggregation in order, aggregate projections (including the implicit `_minmax_count_projection`) and shard pruning work again when the query and the sorting key, partition key or projection use lambdas with the same body, for example `ORDER BY arrayMap(x -> x % b, a)` on a table sorted by that expression. Lambdas with different bodies still do not match. [#131675](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131675) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Faster pipeline startup for merging transforms with many inputs, e.g. reading in order from tables with many parts. [#131683](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131683) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* `dictGetDescendants` and `dictGetChildren` are faster when many keys of a block have children: the set that detects loops in the hierarchy no longer zeroes a buffer sized for the whole block once per such key. The `dictGetDescendants` queries of the `hierarchical_dictionaries` performance test run about 6x faster. [#131775](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131775) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) is faster and uses less memory for `GROUP BY` with many distinct keys at high thread counts: it reduces the overhead of staging records, which grew with the number of threads. On the 12 ClickBench queries with millions of groups, the geometric mean speedup is 1.15x at 192 threads, up to 1.22x, and peak memory drops by 23%. [#131902](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131902) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) is faster for `GROUP BY` with many distinct keys at high thread counts: it reduces the overhead of freeing memory after the merge, which grew with the number of threads. On the 12 ClickBench queries with millions of groups, the geometric mean speedup is 1.16x at 192 threads, up to 1.42x. [#131904](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131904) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) is faster for `GROUP BY` with `ORDER BY count() DESC LIMIT n` over many distinct keys at high thread counts, and the same with `uniqExact` and `uniqExactIf`: it reduces the overhead of the merge of such top-K queries, which grew with the number of threads. On the 12 ClickBench queries with millions of groups, the geometric mean speedup is 1.03x at 192 threads, up to 1.28x. [#131905](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131905) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Faster reading of Parquet `Decimal32` and `Decimal64` values stored as full-width big-endian `FIXED_LEN_BYTE_ARRAY`, as ClickHouse writes them. [#131907](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131907) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* `IcebergLocal` reads Parquet files with the local seek threshold (8 KiB) instead of the remote one (4 MiB), so it no longer reads unneeded bytes between column chunks. [#131914](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131914) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* Faster `PREWHERE` on Parquet files when the passing rows are scattered: the reader now decodes nearby runs of passing rows as one range and drops the rows between them afterwards. [#131935](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131935) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* The adaptive aggregator (`enable_adaptive_aggregator`, enabled by default) is faster for `GROUP BY` queries that return millions of groups at high thread counts: it reduces the overhead of the output of its merge, which grew with the number of threads. Queries over the ClickBench `hits` table that return 20 to 100 million groups run up to 1.29x faster at 64 threads and 1.30x to 2.20x faster at 192 threads. [#131956](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/131956) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* Fixed queries and merges on encrypted disks over remote storage discarding the first prefetch of every column and reading it again synchronously. [#132015](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132015) ([Christoph Wurm](https://github.com/cwurm)).
* A PREWHERE condition with `startsWith` or `endsWith` over a `String`, or with `LIKE` or `ILIKE` with a constant pattern, such as `m['a'] = 'x' AND startsWith(m['b'], 'p-')`, is evaluated in the same read step as the condition before it again, instead of in a separate step that filters the whole block in between. Such queries over a `Map` column are about 1.2 times faster. [#132112](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132112) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The Parquet writer now also ends a data page after `output_format_parquet_data_page_row_limit` rows (new setting, default 20000), so filters on sorted columns can skip pages using the page index. Set it to 0 to cut pages by size only, as before. [#132180](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132180) ([Murphy](https://github.com/murphy-4o)).
* Building the constant of a large literal list such as `x IN (v1, ..., vN)` no longer allocates a 4 KiB buffer per value: a query with 10000 values in the list runs about 17% faster and uses about 38 MiB less memory. [#132227](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132227) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Reading from `MergeTree` is faster when a thread has only a few read tasks, which happens with many threads or when a query reads a small part of a table: the end of the read is now cut into smaller tasks, of 1/32 of the share of a thread and not smaller than `merge_tree_min_read_task_size`, so that the threads finish together. All 43 ClickBench queries together run 1.08x faster at 64 threads and 1.11x faster at 192 threads, and a repeated filter that the query condition cache narrows runs up to 1.5x faster. The new setting `merge_tree_read_end_tasks_per_stream` (32 by default) controls this fraction, and setting it to `0` restores the previous behavior. [#132259](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132259) ([Nihal Z. Miaji](https://github.com/nihalzp)).
* `stringBytesEntropy` is faster on short strings: it now scans only the range of byte values that occur in the string instead of all 256 (about 15 times faster for 1-byte strings, 1.5-2.2 times for short ASCII strings such as numbers, host names and hex hashes). The results are unchanged. [#132415](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132415) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Primary key analysis of a filter that matches many whole key ranges, such as `WHERE flag` over a `UInt8` key column, is faster again with many threads: the per-range check for a `NULL` or `NaN` nested in a `Tuple` key no longer copies the key type, which made threads contend on its reference count. [#132532](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132532) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Backported in [#133927](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133927): `ORDER BY ... LIMIT` whose sorted input blocks are smaller than `max_block_size` re-merges its accumulated blocks again, as in 26.9, instead of switching the re-merge off after its first run, so its peak memory no longer grows with the input. On the query from [#131716](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131716) the peak goes from 1.38 GB back to about 260 MB, as in 26.8. `max_bytes_before_remerge_sort` now counts only the blocks accumulated since the previous re-merge, unless a spill to disk is due. Closes [#131716](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/131716). [#132538](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132538) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Outbound TLS connections no longer resolve the server host name during the handshake when the server certificate names that host. Since hostname verification of server certificates became the default, every new HTTPS connection (for example to a `web` or `s3` disk) did this extra lookup, which slowed down cold queries. [#132627](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132627) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Changes the text index dictionary default block size from 512 to 8192. [#132804](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132804) ([Aaron Knudtson](https://github.com/knudtty)).
* With `make_distributed_plan`, a task combines small blocks before it sends them to other tasks. A query with a selective filter gives many blocks of a few rows, and each of them was sent as a separate packet. [#132817](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/132817) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* Improve filtering performance when reading `Vortex` files with `empty` or `notEmpty` on string columns. [#133022](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/133022) ([Maksim Dergousov](https://github.com/m7kss1)).
* Backported in [#133925](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133925): Pass the size of data lake files to the replicas of a distributed read. [#133820](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/133820) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).

#### Improvement
* Add an opt-in optimization for `if`, `multiIf`, and `transform` with constant string arguments to use `LowCardinality`. [#80263](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/80263) ([Joanna Hulboj](https://github.com/jh0x)).
* If `use_skip_indexes_on_data_read` is `True` and there are usable skip indexes for the predicate(s) and there are projection candidates also, perform skip index filtering before selecting a projection or primary key parts for query execution. [#101526](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/101526) ([Shankar Iyer](https://github.com/shankar-iyer)).
* A column-level grant now covers the subcolumns of that column: `GRANT SELECT(t)` allows `SELECT t.a`, `m.keys`, `arr.size0`, `n.null` and dynamic `JSON` paths, and a revoked column also denies its subcolumns (previously `GRANT SELECT ON t` with `REVOKE SELECT(m)` still allowed `SELECT m.keys`). A grant or revoke on the exact subcolumn name, such as ``GRANT SELECT(`json.a`)``, keeps taking precedence over its column. `EXPLAIN WHATIF` is now denied on tables with a row policy. [#103402](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/103402) ([BoloniniD](https://github.com/BoloniniD)).
* You can now monitor aggregate dictionary state through seven new gauges in `system.asynchronous_metrics`, without querying `system.dictionaries` on every monitoring interval. [#105472](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/105472) ([Taeknology](https://github.com/Taeknology)).
* The error message for an unsupported mix of implicitly and explicitly valued `Enum` elements now describes the actual constraint. [#110986](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/110986) ([Yash ](https://github.com/Onyx2406)).
* Automatic parallel replicas now sample aggregate states from the whole hash table when estimating their size, instead of only a prefix of it, which was biased for `UInt8` and `UInt16` group keys. [#111985](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/111985) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `ALTER TABLE ... MODIFY COLUMN` that changes a column's type in a `MergeTree`-family table now switches the column type and the table's serialization statistics (which choose between sparse and full serialization) in one step, so a part committed while the `ALTER` runs is never mixed into statistics built for the old type. [#114019](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/114019) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Make `INSERT` and background merges fit into the memory of a small server. On a machine with a couple of GiB of RAM, `INSERT ... SELECT` into a table with many columns used to fail with `MEMORY_LIMIT_EXCEEDED` because the write buffers, the squashing stage and the Parquet reader were sized for large servers; and merges of very wide tables such as `system.metric_log` used to fail and never make progress, leaving the table to accumulate parts forever. All of these now scale with `max_server_memory_usage`. New `MergeTree` settings `max_memory_ratio_to_activate_adaptive_write_buffer` and `merge_memory_estimate_per_source_part_column` control the last two; set either to `0` for the previous behavior. [#116083](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116083) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added six columns to `system.blob_storage_log` describing the HTTP connection that carried each object storage request: `connection_id`, `connection_local_port`, `connection_socket_inode`, `connection_requests`, `connection_age_microseconds` and `connection_idle_microseconds`. This makes it possible to see whether a slow request landed on a freshly opened socket or on a keep-alive connection that had been sitting idle in the pool. [#116361](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116361) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Updated `OPTIMIZE TABLE` on Iceberg tables to match position deletes to data files the same way `SELECT` does. Compaction no longer fails with `Position deletes are only supported for data files of Parquet format in Iceberg` when the snapshot contains ORC or Avro data files that no position delete applies to. [#116941](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/116941) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* SCRAM SHA-256 authentication now fails closed for users with a time-based one-time password, because a SCRAM proof cannot carry a second factor. [#117408](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117408) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* When the preemptive CPU scheduler is used (a CPU resource that defines both `MASTER THREAD` and `WORKER THREAD`, with `cpu_slot_preemption`), a query thread now releases its CPU slot while it is blocked on I/O or sleeping with no task to run, instead of holding the slot idle. This lets other queries use the CPU that would otherwise be left idle under the workload concurrency limit, and fixes CPU-slot over-provisioning where a sleeping worker held a slot indefinitely. Because a released slot is re-acquired on wake and a workload's slot usage is reconciled only at processor step boundaries, a workload can briefly run more concurrent threads than its configured CPU concurrency limit while this is enabled. Controlled by the new `cpu_slot_parking` server setting (enabled by default). [#117711](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/117711) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* `clickhouse-local` and `clickhouse-server` no longer take an extra minute to exit when the last operation before shutdown created, dropped or renamed an access entity (a user, role, settings profile, row policy, quota or masking policy) in the `local_directory` access storage. [#118099](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118099) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Statements that change only metadata, such as `DROP`, `DETACH`, `TRUNCATE`, `RENAME`, `GRANT`, `REVOKE`, `SYSTEM`, `CREATE TABLE` with columns, and `ALTER` that changes the schema, are now exempt from workload scheduling (query-slot and memory-reservation admission, CPU and IO), so they do not queue behind regular queries. The server-wide `max_concurrent_queries*` limits still apply. Previously they were scheduled under the `workload` setting like any other query. [#118419](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/118419) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* Plan-based parallel replicas can now distribute a join whose broadcast side reads `system.one`, `numbers(...)`, `system.numbers` or `generate_series(...)`, instead of keeping the join on the initiator. [#119862](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119862) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* The `max_waiting_queries` server setting is now enforced. A query that blocks while a table, a database, or the distributed DDL worker it needs is still loading or starting up asynchronously is counted, and a query that would exceed the limit is refused with `TOO_MANY_SIMULTANEOUS_QUERIES` instead of joining the waiters. The new `WaitingQuery` metric reports how many queries are waiting. The setting's documentation no longer claims that waiting queries are excluded from the `max_concurrent_*` limits, which was never implemented. Closes [#118663](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/118663). [#119916](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/119916) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Improved `automatic_parallel_replicas` observability by adding outcome-specific `ProfileEvents`, split optimization/plan-build timing counters, and runtime statistics-collection timing in `query_log`. [#120884](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120884) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* The two `FILE_DOESNT_EXIST` exceptions raised while resolving an Iceberg table's metadata file, and the one raised for a Paimon table's schema, now report their evidence. Where nothing under the listed prefix matched, the message reports what that prefix held: the entry count, and up to 10 entry names with their modification times. For Iceberg it also reports how many listing attempts spanned how long, and, where candidates were found but all rejected, their count, how many were temporary commit files, and their names. The two Iceberg sites previously emitted identical text. [#120954](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/120954) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `UNDROP TABLE ... UUID` that names a database other than the one the table was dropped from now fails with `UNKNOWN_TABLE` instead of a `LOGICAL_ERROR` exception. [#121121](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121121) ([Ilias](https://github.com/iliasabk)).
* With `parallel_replicas_min_number_of_rows_per_replica`, collecting the filters of a query no longer plans its `IN` subqueries with parallel replicas, which saves an extra index analysis of the subquery's read and an unused parallel replicas coordinator. [#121273](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121273) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SELECT DISTINCT`, `GROUP BY` and `LIMIT BY` over a `Distributed` table whose sharding key contains `arrayJoin` now decline the `optimize_distributed_group_by_sharding_key` shortcut and merge on the initiator. Previously the query planner reached a branch marked unreachable, which is `abort()` in a debug or sanitizer build. [#121495](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121495) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Fix `modulo` throwing `Division by the most negative number` when the divisor is a constant equal to the minimum value of a signed integer type (e.g. `modulo(materialize(5), toInt8(-128))`). It now returns the correct remainder. [#121645](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121645) ([Shubham](https://github.com/shubhamojha1)).
* The BigLake catalog of the `DataLakeCatalog` database engine can authenticate with a Google service account key (setting `google_service_account_key`), which works outside Google Cloud without a user refresh token. [#121749](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121749) ([Nikita Fomichev](https://github.com/fm4v)).
* Filter push-down no longer leaves a stale `Nullable(UInt8)` result type on an `and` predicate after a Nullable conjunct has been pushed out of it. Queries planned with `make_distributed_plan = 1` that previously failed with `Deserialized function and has invalid type` now run. [#121942](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/121942) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* AI functions issue provider requests concurrently from a server-wide thread pool (`ai_function_max_thread_pool_size`), with up to `ai_function_max_concurrent_requests_per_thread` requests in flight per query thread (default 8). `ai_function_max_retries` defaults to 3, and new `ProfileEvents` count retried, failed and throttled provider requests. [#122484](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122484) ([George Larionov](https://github.com/george-larionov)).
* Add AI function request time, retry backoff time, and prompt-cache token counts to `ProfileEvents`. Include cached Anthropic input tokens in total input usage and query quotas. [#122559](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122559) ([Ravi Kumar L](https://github.com/upman)).
* The watch set by a Keeper list request that returns children stats or data (`FilteredListWithStatsAndData` or `ListWithOptions` with `with_stat` or `with_data`) now also fires when the data of a direct child that passes the request's filter, or the ACL of any direct child, changes, as a children-changed event on the listed path. Closes [#118774](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/118774). [#122750](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122750) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Merge selection now treats a part whose modification time is later than the current time as a fresh part instead of a very old one. Such a part (committed while merges were being selected, loaded or attached with a future directory modification time, or seen after the system clock moved back) could be merged right away, and with `min_age_to_force_merge_on_partition_only` it could make its partition look old enough to be force-merged in full. [#122777](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122777) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* PromQL: an operator on two constant numbers, such as `1./6.`, now gives a constant, so functions that need a constant parameter accept it, for example `histogram_quantile(1./6., ...)`. [#122781](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122781) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* The Prometheus HTTP API endpoint `/api/v1/query_range` now rejects a query if `(end - start) / step` is greater than the new setting `promql_max_points_per_series` (default 11000), as Prometheus does. Only the HTTP `query_range` endpoint is limited; the `prometheusQueryRange` table function is not. Set the setting to 0 to lift the limit. [#122812](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122812) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* A skip index materialized over a JSON subcolumn that was added as a lazy type hint (`enable_json_lazy_type_hints`) without rewriting the column data can now be used for query pruning. [#122846](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122846) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* Errors that the Prometheus query API raises before running the query, such as an unknown or malformed setting, failed authentication or an unknown role, are now returned as a Prometheus JSON error whose `errorType` matches the HTTP status, instead of plain text. [#122898](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/122898) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Join reordering can now estimate the rows of an Iceberg table from the `record_count` of its data files in the manifest files, after partition and min/max pruning by the query filter, without reading data. Enable it with the new setting `use_iceberg_manifest_statistics` (disabled by default). [#123085](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123085) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* Allow `Distributed`, `Remote`, `RemoteSecure`, and non-persistent `Set` and `Join` tables in `Replicated` databases when `database_replicated_allow_only_replicated_engine` is enabled. Continue rejecting writable non-replicated `MergeTree` tables using local or remote storage policies, and reject server `File` tables backed by explicit paths. [#123493](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123493) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Add support for `ALTER TABLE ${TABLE} MODIFY SETTING` of data lake tables. [#123519](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123519) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* With `use_iceberg_manifest_statistics`, join reordering also uses the number of distinct values, min/max and NULL fraction of Iceberg columns, estimated from the manifest metrics of the data files without reading data, and estimates the filter, `PREWHERE` and row policy of an Iceberg read on them, as for MergeTree tables with column statistics. The setting now requires `use_statistics`. [#123553](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123553) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* `BACKUP` of a `Memory` table now stops on `KILL QUERY` and `max_execution_time` while it serializes the table's data, instead of serializing all of it first. [#123627](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/123627) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Requests to an AWS Glue catalog are now retried when Glue answers with a transient HTTP status (for example `InternalServiceException`, HTTP 500), so a single transient Glue failure no longer fails `SHOW TABLES` or other queries on a `DataLakeCatalog` database. [#124132](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124132) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `KILL QUERY` and `max_execution_time` now interrupt query analysis while it hashes query tree nodes. A query such as `SELECT * FROM (SELECT * FROM t) GROUP BY ALL` over a table with thousands of columns could keep being analyzed for minutes after it was killed or timed out. [#124376](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124376) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The Iceberg row estimate for join reordering (`use_iceberg_manifest_statistics`) now subtracts the rows removed by deletion vectors and by position delete files that apply to a single data file. [#124395](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124395) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* Added setting `use_skip_indexes_for_disjunctions_max_rpn_elements` to control the maximum size of filters with mixed `AND` and `OR` conditions evaluated with skip indexes. The default limit is raised from 32 to 128 elements, e.g. from 16 to 64 conditions on different columns connected by `OR`. [#124454](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124454) ([Shankar Iyer](https://github.com/shankar-iyer)).
* Queries with `FINAL` now use the virtual row optimization of reading in order. Supported for `ReplacingMergeTree`, `CollapsingMergeTree`, `VersionedCollapsingMergeTree`, `SummingMergeTree`, `AggregatingMergeTree` and `CoalescingMergeTree`. [#124468](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124468) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)).
* When Keeper accepts connections but does not answer, the queries and background operations that need a new Keeper session now share one connection attempt instead of making their attempts one after another, so the K-th of them no longer waits about K attempts, and `zookeeperSessionUptime` and `SYSTEM RECONNECT ZOOKEEPER` no longer wait for the connection. A query that waits for the attempt stops on `KILL QUERY`, `max_execution_time` and `get_zookeeper_lock_acquire_timeout_ms`. [#124483](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124483) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With `run_query_in_background = 1`, errors raised before the query starts its main work are now returned to the client instead of only being recorded in `system.query_log`. Examples are an unknown table, a missing grant, a table that exceeds `max_table_size_to_drop`, or invalid `ALTER`/`OPTIMIZE`/`DELETE`/`BACKUP` arguments. [#124713](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124713) ([Miсhael Stetsyuk](https://github.com/mstetsyuk)).
* An unregistered engine name in `CREATE TABLE` or `CREATE DATABASE` (for example `ENGINE = memory`, `ENGINE = atomic` or the table-function spelling `ENGINE = s3(...)`) is now reported as `UNKNOWN_STORAGE` / `UNKNOWN_DATABASE_ENGINE` with the "Maybe you meant" hint for users without a wildcard `TABLE ENGINE` grant, instead of `ACCESS_DENIED` for a `TABLE ENGINE ON <name>` grant that cannot be given. [#124753](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124753) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Index analysis now keeps an exact condition for `IN`, `NOT IN` and `has` on a `Tuple` column under an injective key expression such as `ORDER BY toString(k)`, so `NOT IN` and `NOT has` can skip parts and granules. `toString` of `Time`, `Time64`, `DateTime64` and of a `QBit` of floating-point elements is no longer treated as injective, since distinct values can render to the same string (a `Time` beyond `999:59:59`, a `DateTime64` past `9999-12-31`, `NaN` payloads), so `GROUP BY`, `uniq*` and index analysis no longer rely on that claim for them. [#124831](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124831) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* With `use_iceberg_manifest_statistics`, the Cascades optimizer estimates Iceberg reads from the manifest files, as join reordering does, instead of treating them as empty. [#124857](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124857) ([Antonio Filipovic](https://github.com/antoniofilipovic)).
* The read-in-order optimization now works through joins that can spill to disk: when the query plan needs the left table's row order (e.g. `ORDER BY t1.pk LIMIT n`), a hash join that crosses the spill threshold spills through a partial merge join, which preserves the order, instead of a grace hash join. join_algorithm = 'partial_merge' got an order-preserving probe and supports the optimization too. [#124873](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124873) ([János Benjamin Antal](https://github.com/antaljanosbenjamin)).
* Allow subcolumns, such as `Tuple` elements and `JSON` typed paths, as the version and `is_deleted` columns of `ReplacingMergeTree` and as the sign column of `CollapsingMergeTree`, for example `ReplacingMergeTree(meta.ver, meta.deleted)`. Also fix `ALTER TABLE ... MODIFY COLUMN` of the sign column of `CollapsingMergeTree`, which rejected even modifications that keep its `Int8` type. [#124923](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124923) ([Pavel Kruglov](https://github.com/Avogar)).
* Added the `access_control_improvements.forbid_sql_access_entities_targeting_users_config` setting to block SQL-created quotas, row policies and settings profiles from applying to users defined in `users.xml`. [#124940](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/124940) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* `CREATE TABLE` without engine arguments in a `DataLakeCatalog` database with an Iceberg REST catalog now lets the catalog choose the table location and write the first metadata file, instead of ClickHouse writing it to storage directly. This makes such creates work with `vended_credentials = 1`. [#125006](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125006) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)).
* Negative and fractional `LIMIT`/`OFFSET`, `GROUPING SETS` and window functions with `PARTITION BY` now wait until a downstream step asks for data before reading their input, so a lightweight `UPDATE` or `DELETE` whose `ignore(...)` predicate holds an `IN` subquery over a `MATERIALIZED` CTE no longer fails with a logical error. [#125043](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125043) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `SYSTEM FLUSH LOGS` now also waits for profiler and memory samples that are still on their way to `system.trace_log`, so the samples taken before the flush are visible right after it. [#125044](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125044) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `cassandra` dictionary source uses a new built-in client of the Cassandra native protocol instead of the DataStax C++ driver. It supports TLS (the `secure` setting) and compression (the `compression` setting), reads `boolean` and `timeuuid` columns, and the `query` setting now works. [#125093](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125093) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Prometheus query API requests (`/api/v1/query`, `/api/v1/query_range`) record the PromQL expression in `system.query_log.log_comment` unless the request sets its own `log_comment`. [#125225](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125225) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* Prometheus protocol handlers and `prometheusQuery` now log per-request lines and the generated SQL at the `debug` level instead of `information`, so a server running at `information` no longer writes a log line for every remote write, scrape or PromQL query. [#125236](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125236) ([Valery Petrov](https://github.com/valerypetrov)).
* The Paimon incremental read (`paimon_incremental_read = 1`) now fails with `INVALID_STATE` instead of silently skipping snapshots when the warehouse was rewound and the writer reused the snapshot id that the Keeper cursor points at. [#125257](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125257) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* An Iceberg commit (for example `ALTER TABLE ... EXECUTE expire_snapshots`) whose new metadata file the storage refuses to write, such as `Permission denied` on a local directory or S3 `AccessDenied`, now fails at once with that error instead of being retried as a commit conflict until `LIMIT_EXCEEDED`. [#125406](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125406) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Introduce new setting `max_bytes_to_buffer_for_packed_part` which limits maximum in-memory buffer size for packed size. The rest data is spilled to disk and uploaded to destination storage as a single packed part. [#125468](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125468) ([alesapin](https://github.com/alesapin)).
* The `clickhouse_json` dialect and `formatQueryFromJSON` now reject a TTL element that has a `where_expr` with a mode other than `DELETE` (`TO DISK`/`TO VOLUME`, `RECOMPRESS`, `GROUP BY`) with `BAD_ARGUMENTS`, as SQL parsing does, instead of storing table metadata that cannot be parsed at the next server start. [#125475](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125475) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Azure Blob Storage listings now skip the directory entries of an account with a hierarchical namespace (ADLS Gen2), so `ALTER TABLE ... EXECUTE remove_orphan_files` on Iceberg tables there only deletes files and no longer reports a failed deletion for every directory. Closes [#125462](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125462). [#125500](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125500) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The web terminal (`/webterminal`) now applies the same limits as other interfaces: `max_sessions_for_user` is checked before a session is started, an idle session is closed after `idle_connection_timeout`, and `receive_timeout` and `send_timeout` are applied to the WebSocket connection. [#125514](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125514) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Add automatic upgrade to SHA256 for S3-compatible `DeleteObjects` when MD5 fails. New `delete_checksum_algorithm` setting allows explicit algorithm configuration. [#125566](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125566) ([Craig Ringer](https://github.com/ringerc)).
* On macOS, the sampling query profiler and `system.stack_trace` no longer interrupt threads with signals; they stop threads briefly with `thread_suspend` and read their stacks directly. The `pthread_rwlock` and libc time zone workarounds this makes unnecessary are removed. [#125609](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125609) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* ... [#125615](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125615) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Write `split_offsets` of Parquet data files to Iceberg manifests. [#125619](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125619) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Reduce the size of the positions index size up to ~30% and make phrase search decodes faster ~10-30%. One major perf improvement is decoding positions of rare tokens is significantly faster. [#125630](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125630) ([Elmi Ahmadov](https://github.com/ahmadov)).
* Iceberg bucket split by split offsets. Do not use parquet metadata anymore - let's split file by split offsets from metadata. [#125636](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125636) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Added the server setting `totp_track_used_codes_in_keeper`. When enabled, codes of time-based one-time passwords are accepted at most once across all servers that share the same ZooKeeper or ClickHouse Keeper (previously, at most once per server), and the record of used codes survives server restarts. The accepted codes are tracked in Keeper under the path set by `totp_used_codes_keeper_path` (default `/clickhouse/access/one_time_passwords`); while Keeper is unavailable, authentication with a one-time password fails. [#125664](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125664) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)).
* The `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` input formats read struct columns whose fields have empty or repeated names as an unnamed `Tuple` instead of failing schema inference. [#125674](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125674) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* Reduced the memory usage of incremental `BACKUP`: the base backup keeps only the sizes and checksums of its files instead of the full information about each file. For a base backup of millions of files this saves gigabytes. [#125717](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125717) ([Julia Kartseva](https://github.com/jkartseva)).
* Aggregate functions that don't support the `Variant` type natively, such as `sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`, `quantile`, can now be applied to `Variant` arguments. They are calculated over `Nullable` of the least common supertype of the alternatives. Previously such queries threw an exception like `Illegal type Variant(...) of argument for aggregate function sum`. [#125726](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125726) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Made the socket buffers of streaming exchanges configurable; they now default to kernel TCP autotuning instead of fixed sizes that capped throughput on high-RTT links. [#125727](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125727) ([zoomxi](https://github.com/zoomxi)).
* Text index searches through the userspace page cache no longer reset a remote connection on almost every cache miss: the text index files that are read out of file order now use a page cache lookahead of one block. The new setting `use_page_cache_lookahead_for_text_index` restores the normal lookahead. [#125770](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125770) ([Antonio Andelic](https://github.com/antonio2368)).
* Compare TOTP codes and `plaintext_password` passwords in constant time, so that the response time does not depend on how many leading characters are correct or on which time window matched. [#125785](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125785) ([Vladimir Cherkasov](https://github.com/vdimir)).
* `EXPLAIN WHATIF` now estimates hypothetical projections with a `WHERE` clause. [#125792](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125792) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* Instead of having one thread per FileLog table to watch files changes, use a single thread to watch file changes for all tables. This should eliminate thread starvation bugs that happens when we have too many FileLog tables. ... [#125794](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125794) ([Muhammad Soliman](https://github.com/mu-soliman)).
* A replica that takes part in a parallel replicas read now follows the initiator even when its own `automatic_parallel_replicas_mode` is not 0, for example when `allow_feature_tier` drops the value the initiator sends, or when `INSERT ... SETTINGS automatic_parallel_replicas_mode = 2 SELECT` re-applies it on the replica. Previously such a replica read its whole table on its own, so a query could count rows more than once and a distributed `INSERT SELECT` wrote them once per replica. [#125801](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125801) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* An inequality join (`join_algorithm = 'ie_join'`) now uses column statistics to pick its most selective conditions only when those statistics actually describe the rows being joined. Otherwise it uses its conditions in the order they are written, so misleading estimates no longer drive the choice. - Value ranges of grouping keys now carry through `GROUP BY`, which removes duplicate rows but keeps every key value. - They also carry through `ORDER BY … LIMIT n`, including reads that use the top-K optimization, when the input is known to have at most `n` rows. - Statistics are no longer taken to describe the rows in these cases: - after a filter (including `PREWHERE` and row policies), `FINAL`, `SAMPLE`, any other `LIMIT`, `ARRAY JOIN`, or another join; - when the read skips lightweight-deleted rows or rows superseded by a `UNIQUE KEY`; - when the read applies mutations or patch parts on the fly, including a pending `RENAME COLUMN` / `DROP COLUMN`, or masking policies; - for `GROUPING SETS` and `WITH TOTALS` overflow rows; - when a read, sort or `GROUP BY` limit has a non-throwing overflow mode; - for Iceberg delete files; - when only some parts or data lake files have statistics or bounds. - Column statistics left outdated by `ALTER TABLE … MODIFY COLUMN` are no longer used for queries without a filter, matching the existing behavior for filtered queries. [#125809](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125809) ([Christoph Viebig](https://github.com/cv4g)).
* `system.crash_log.fault_access_type` and the `Access: ...` part of the fatal log message now report read or write for `SIGSEGV` and `SIGBUS` on Linux aarch64. Until now, they were always empty there, meaning production ARM crashes lacked this information. On x86_64, signals sent with `kill`, `tgkill`, or `sigqueue` no longer have their access type set. [#125840](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125840) ([Miсhael Stetsyuk](https://github.com/mstetsyuk)).
* `clickhouse-benchmark` supports an open-loop mode: with `--arrival-rate` queries arrive at random times at a fixed average rate (a Poisson process), whatever the server does, with at most `--max-in-flight` queries executing at the same time. A list of rates runs a sweep with one report per rate, `--seed` makes the arrival schedule reproducible, and `--per-query` prints statistics for every query. [#125921](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125921) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* The "Format" button in the Web UI now supports inputs with multiple queries: each query is formatted separately and replaced in place, keeping the comments and delimiters between them. [#125939](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125939) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The number of threads of a query with an automatic `max_threads` is chosen once per query: all its parts (subqueries, sets, read streams of joined tables, pipeline resizes) are planned with the same free memory (`max_threads_min_free_memory_per_thread`) and the same fair share of CPU slots (`use_concurrency_control_for_max_threads`). [#125946](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125946) ([Sergei Trifonov](https://github.com/serxa)).
* Fixed a misleading error `Storage for table ... does not exist, but is included in replication stream` logged by a `MaterializedPostgreSQL` database with `materialized_postgresql_schema_list` or `materialized_postgresql_tables_list_with_schema` after `DETACH TABLE ... PERMANENTLY`. [#125952](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125952) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* A `MaterializedPostgreSQL` database or table can no longer be created with both a user-managed `materialized_postgresql_replication_slot` and `materialized_postgresql_use_unique_replication_consumer_identifier`, because a user-managed slot cannot be unique per consumer. Existing databases and tables with this combination continue to load. [#125963](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125963) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` input formats can read files with repeated column names: a repeated name gets a numeric suffix, for example `ints_1`. [#125994](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/125994) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* Automatic parallel replicas settings (`automatic_parallel_replicas_mode`, `automatic_parallel_replicas_min_bytes_per_replica`) are now production tier instead of experimental. [#126005](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126005) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* The `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` input formats can read the `ListView` and `LargeListView` types as `Array`. [#126014](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126014) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* The Parquet reader now rejects a file whose schema has an out-of-range physical type, repetition type or converted type with an `INCORRECT_DATA` error when it reads the footer, instead of reading an invalid repetition type as `OPTIONAL` and failing later with a misleading error. [#126025](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126025) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The nodes in the output of `EXPLAIN AST graph = 1` are numbered sequentially instead of being identified by memory addresses, so the output is the same on every run. [#126031](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126031) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Fatal error reports no longer contain runtime memory addresses: stack frames and the fault address are printed as offsets inside the executable or a library, which can be passed to `addr2line`, and other addresses are hidden. `system.crash_log.fault_address` now contains the offset inside the executable, or `NULL` when the address is elsewhere. [#126039](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126039) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Fixed `intExp10` returning nonsense for integer arguments with magnitude >= 2^31, for example `intExp10(4294967297)` returned `10` and `intExp10(2147483648)` returned `0`. It now saturates the same way `intExp2` does: arguments >= 20 return the maximum `UInt64` value and negative arguments return `0`. [#126109](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126109) ([PRINCE KUMAR MAURYA](https://github.com/alwaysprince05)).
* In `Pretty` formats, the ellipsis of a value cut by `output_format_pretty_max_value_width` is no longer underlined when the cut falls inside highlighted trailing whitespace or a highlighted digit group. Closes [#104834](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/104834). [#126146](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126146) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `GROUP BY` keys computed by different expressions of the same type, for example `intDiv(a, 1000000)` and `a + 1` in `SELECT k FROM (SELECT ... AS k FROM t) GROUP BY k`, no longer share one hash table size statistics entry, so an aggregation preallocates its hash table for its own number of groups instead of for another query's. Closes [#125826](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/125826). [#126148](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126148) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Removed exclamation marks from error messages. [#126178](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126178) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* The reason phrase of an HTTP error response from a remote server is now scrubbed of the request's bearer token in the error message, as the response body already is. [#126180](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126180) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Credentials in the URL of a `url` wildcard expanded from HTTP index pages (`allow_url_wildcard_from_index_pages`), or of a `web_index` disk, are now masked in the errors about an index page redirected to a different origin and about an index page over `max_http_index_page_size`. [#126184](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126184) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* A `JOIN` whose `ON` condition has a constant-false conjunct now works with the experimental `parallel_replicas_plan_based` setting when the join uses `grace_hash`, spills to disk, or runs with `join_use_nulls = 1`, instead of terminating the server or failing with `NOT_FOUND_COLUMN_IN_BLOCK`. [#126204](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126204) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` input formats can read the Arrow interval types: `YEAR_MONTH` as `IntervalMonth`, and `DAY_TIME` and `MONTH_DAY_NANO` as a `Tuple` of intervals. [#126206](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126206) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* The `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` input formats can read `RunEndEncoded` columns. [#126207](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126207) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* Reduce the number of S3 requests when reading Iceberg tables by reusing metadata lookups and caching parsed `metadata.json` files: one GET and one LIST instead of three GETs and two LISTs. [#126267](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126267) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Update the dependency `apache/datasketches-cpp` to release 5.3.0. [#126268](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126268) ([Christoph Viebig](https://github.com/cv4g)).
* Simplify sorting description parsing in iceberg descriptions. [#126273](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126273) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* A distributed `INSERT ... SELECT ... SETTINGS automatic_parallel_replicas_mode = ...` now uses parallel replicas, as it does when the setting comes from the session or profile. Previously the setting in the SELECT's `SETTINGS` clause silently made the insert run on the initiator alone. [#126317](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126317) ([Nikita Taranov](https://github.com/nickitat)).
* Range conditions such as `x < 100` on a column with `tdigest` statistics are now estimated correctly on tables with several parts. Previously the estimate depended on the order of the parts and could be zero, so the planner could pick a worse join order, PREWHERE order or distributed plan. [#126385](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126385) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Add upper bound to snapshot message transfer. [#126480](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126480) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* PostgreSQL protocol connections now reject unsupported client encodings instead of advertising an encoding that does not match returned text bytes. [#126487](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126487) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fix misaligned table borders in `Pretty` formats when `output_format_pretty_max_value_width` and `output_format_pretty_max_column_pad_width` are set to huge values. [#126534](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126534) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fix huge values of the `PerfMinEnabledTime` and `PerfMinEnabledRunningTime` profile events after the list of perf events changed between queries on the same thread or a perf counter could not be read. [#126538](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126538) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fix a possible crash or out-of-bounds memory access when reading from HDFS if a DataNode or NameNode sends malformed responses. [#126546](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126546) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Fixed `CREATE TABLE` and `ALTER TABLE` accepting a MergeTree table (with `flatten_nested = 0`) whose non-flattened `Nested` column, named with the prefix of a flattened `Nested` group, writes its streams into the files of other columns. Parts inserted into such a table could not be read. [#126557](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126557) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The native Arrow IPC reader can read big-endian Arrow files and streams. [#126562](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126562) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* Schema inference for the `Arrow` and `ArrowStream` formats infers columns of the Arrow `null` type as `Nullable(Nothing)` instead of failing, and reports a clear error when every column is skipped because of `input_format_arrow_skip_columns_with_unsupported_types_in_schema_inference`. [#126563](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126563) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* The `.pq` file extension is recognized as `Parquet`, so `INTO OUTFILE 'x.pq'` and `file('x.pq')` write Parquet without an explicit format. [#126565](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126565) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* A UTF-8 BOM at the beginning of a `CSV`, `TSV` or `CustomSeparated` file is now skipped when the header is detected automatically (which is enabled by default). Previously, the BOM became a part of the first column name, and the query could fail with `CANNOT_PARSE_INPUT_ASSERTION_FAILED`. [#126591](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126591) ([Adegbite Ayoade Abel](https://github.com/tripleaceme)).
* Fixed `DECIMAL_OVERFLOW` thrown by `=`, `!=`, `IN`, `NOT IN` and non-`Nullable` `IS [NOT] DISTINCT FROM` when a decimal is compared with a decimal constant that does not fit its type at its scale, e.g. `d IN (toDecimal128(1000000000, 0))` for `d Decimal(38, 30)`, on sorting and partition keys, `minmax` and `set` indexes, `EmbeddedRocksDB` keys and unindexed columns. Such a constant now equals no value of the column instead of throwing. [#126603](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126603) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Compiling a WebAssembly module for a UDF no longer blocks saving or deleting other modules in `system.webassembly_modules`. [#126605](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126605) ([Vasily Chekalkin](https://github.com/bacek)).
* `clickhouse-client` and `clickhouse-local` stop quietly with exit code 0, without a `Broken pipe` exception, when the reader of their output goes away, e.g. `clickhouse-local -q '...' | head -1`. [#126638](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126638) ([davidrz15](https://github.com/davidrz15)).
* The request body of a SQL-defined HTTP handler (`CREATE HANDLER`) is appended to the handler's query only as the data of an `INSERT`, so it can never extend the handler's SQL. The `_request_body` query parameter of custom HTTP handlers can no longer be passed in the URL. [#126642](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126642) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Added Keeper setting `snapshot_transfer_remote_disk_streaming` to send remote-disk snapshots to followers without keeping the whole file in memory. [#126659](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126659) ([Samuel Krempaský](https://github.com/NIKTONIKTO717)).
* This patch changes the `EXPLAIN AST` or `EXPLAIN SYNTAX` to the analyzer interpreter. [#126676](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126676) ([Mikhail Artemenko](https://github.com/Michicosun)).
* Raise default `iceberg_manifest_decode_concurrency` from 4 to 16. [#126700](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126700) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Adds a minimum threshold for spilling in-memory data when external aggregation is enabled. [#126756](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126756) ([Mikhail Artemenko](https://github.com/Michicosun)).
* System tables no longer contain runtime memory addresses: stack frames outside the main binary are stored as 0, `system.trace_log.ptr` is an identifier instead of an address, and the ids in `system.processors_profile_log` are indices within the query. [#126760](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126760) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Support the `AWS_ENDPOINT_URL_STS` environment variable for S3 web identity credentials, and use the `.amazonaws.eu` STS endpoint in `eusc-*` regions. [#126761](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126761) ([Asya Shneerson](https://github.com/asya-ch)).
* Backported in [#133878](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/133878): Improve parquet small string decoding. [#126776](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126776) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* With the experimental `automatic_parallel_replicas_mode`, a recursive CTE member that orders or groups by an integer constant of its projection (for example `SELECT 5 AS c, k FROM t ORDER BY ALL`) no longer fails with `Positional argument number 5 is out of bounds`. [#126813](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126813) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Implemented `SYSTEM STOP REPLICATED DDL QUERIES` / `SYSTEM START REPLICATED DDL QUERIES` (temporarily prohibit DDL queries in `Replicated` databases and distributed `ON CLUSTER` DDL queries), which previously failed with "Unknown type". `SYSTEM STOP/START VIRTUAL PARTS UPDATE` and `SYSTEM STOP/START REDUCE BLOCKING PARTS` no longer fail. [#126870](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126870) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* When a `MergeTree` table whose settings no longer pass the sanity check (for example, after `background_pool_size` was lowered) is loaded on server startup or `ATTACH`, a warning is logged instead of the problem being silently ignored. [#126875](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126875) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* `ORDER BY ... WITH FILL` is supported in distributed query plans built by the Cascades optimizer. [#126915](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126915) ([Alexander Gololobov](https://github.com/davenger)).
* The rule-based distributed query plan (`make_distributed_plan`) now chooses the number of read tasks from the rows to read and the number of workers (`distributed_plan_default_reader_bucket_count = 0` by default, sized by the new setting `distributed_plan_min_rows_per_reader`), removes the exchange between a read and a partial aggregation or sort, and caps `max_threads` on stateless workers at their number of CPU cores. [#126945](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126945) ([Shankar Iyer](https://github.com/shankar-iyer)).
* `SYSTEM DROP REPLICA` and `SYSTEM DROP DATABASE REPLICA ... WITH TABLES` reject replica names containing `/`. [#126972](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126972) ([Nikolay Degterinsky](https://github.com/evillique)).
* The Azure table functions and engines (`azureBlobStorage`, `icebergAzure`, `AzureBlobStorage`, ...) now reject a malformed URL in the `(url, sas_token)` form with `BAD_ARGUMENTS` instead of failing with `std::out_of_range` or a logical error, and a URL that ends right after the host is parsed like the same URL with a trailing slash. [#126984](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126984) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `EXPLAIN WHATIF` estimates a hypothetical skip index from a sample of index windows when the scan would read more than `index_scan_budget_rows` rows (10 million by default). It shows the sampling error as `marks_span`. [#126991](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/126991) ([Yaroslav Briukhovetskyi](https://github.com/yariks5s)).
* A query that reads with parallel replicas now uses one implementation throughout: a subquery whose `SETTINGS` set `parallel_replicas_plan_based` differently from the query is rejected, instead of silently reading that subquery without parallel replicas. [#127038](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127038) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* Improve glue catalog cache. Let's cache only mapping column name -> timestamp, not the whole metadata. [#127043](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127043) ([Konstantin Vedernikov](https://github.com/scanhex12)).
* Allow `uniq` and `uniq_v2` column statistics on `UUID` columns (including `Nullable(UUID)` and `LowCardinality(UUID)`), so the number of distinct values is available to the planner for UUID keys. [#127053](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127053) ([Antonio Álvarez Caballero](https://github.com/29antonioac)).
* Queries using `additional_table_filters` no longer lose parallel replicas when `parallel_replicas_plan_based` is enabled. [#127058](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127058) ([Igor Nikonov](https://github.com/devcrafter)).
* A table with the `S3`, `AzureBlobStorage`, `HDFS`, `Iceberg*`, `DeltaLake`, `DeltaLakeS3`, `DeltaLakeAzure`, `Hudi`, `Paimon`, `PaimonS3`, `PaimonAzure` or `PaimonHDFS` engine is now read over the replicas of `cluster_for_parallel_replicas` when parallel replicas are enabled, as the table functions of these engines already are with `parallel_replicas_for_cluster_engines`. A replica takes part only if it has the table. The new setting `parallel_replicas_for_object_storage_tables` turns it off. Closes [#127052](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127052). [#127138](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127138) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `CAST` can now convert `Point`, `LineString` and `Polygon` to `MultiPoint`, `MultiLineString` and `MultiPolygon`; the result is a collection that contains the value as its only element. This also works inside `Array`, `Tuple`, `Map`, `Variant` and `Dynamic`, and for `ALTER TABLE ... MODIFY COLUMN`. [#127140](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127140) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* `CAST` from `Variant` and `Dynamic` now fails only if the column has values of a type that cannot be converted to the target type. For example, a `Geometry` column holding only `MultiPolygon` values can now be cast to `MultiPolygon`. Closes [#127110](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/issues/127110). [#127142](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127142) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* When a client cancels a query over the native protocol or disconnects, the query is now also marked as cancelled in the process list, as `KILL QUERY` does. Work that checks only that, such as `sleep`, now stops promptly, including in the remote part of a distributed, `*Cluster` table function or parallel replicas query whose initiator was cancelled, and in an `INSERT` whose client cancels it or disconnects while still sending data. [#127149](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127149) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* Primary key analysis can now prune granules for `divide` and `intDiv` by a `Decimal` constant when the dividend rescale cannot overflow, for example `WHERE price / toDecimal64(3, 2) > 5` on `ORDER BY price`. [#127187](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127187) ([Raúl Marín](https://github.com/Algunenano)).
* Merging states of the `contingency`, `cramersV`, `cramersVBiasCorrected` and `theilsU` aggregate functions whose total row count does not fit into `UInt64` (for example, when multiplying a state by a huge constant) now throws an exception instead of producing an inconsistent state. [#127196](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127196) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
* The statistical functions backed by Boost.Math (`studentTTest`, `studentTTestOneSample`, `welchTTest`, `meanZTest`, `minSampleSizeConversion` and others) return bit-identical results on x86_64 and aarch64: they are evaluated in `double` instead of `long double`, and the SSE2 Lanczos approximation is no longer used. The last digit of some results may differ from previous versions. [#127215](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127215) ([Konstantin Bogdanov](https://github.com/thevar1able)).
* The `obfuscate` table function and the `clickhouse obfuscator` tool now support `LowCardinality`, `Enum`, `Date32`, `DateTime64`, `Decimal`, wide integers, `IPv4`, `IPv6`, `BFloat16`, `Tuple` and `Map` columns. The exception for an unsupported type now names the type and the column. [#127295](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127295) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Hardening: certificate chains containing the compromised eMudhra intermediate CAs or the roots that issued them (`emSign Root TLS CA - G3`, `emSign ECC Root CA - G3`) are rejected even if the trust store of the operating system still trusts them. The CA certificates embedded into the binary (used only when the system has none) are updated from the Chromium root store. There is no security issue in typical use cases. [#127318](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127318) ([Alexey Milovidov](https://github.com/alexey-milovidov)).
* Automatic format detection no longer detects binary data (data with a zero byte) as `TSV` or `CSV`, so a binary file in an unsupported format, such as a `.dbf` file, fails with `CANNOT_DETECT_FORMAT` instead of being read as meaningless text columns. [#127342](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127342) ([Jay Pokale](https://github.com/JayPokale)).
* `KILL QUERY` and `max_execution_time` now stop an `INSERT` into a `MergeTree` table while it writes the columns of a large block, not only between blocks. Before, such an `INSERT` (for example with a small `index_granularity` or with projections) could keep running for minutes after being cancelled. [#127354](https://github.com/ClickHouse/ClickHouse/pull/127354) ([Groene AI](https://github.com/groeneai)).
